### 6.z Homeowner profiles (owner decision D10; modules 77-79; migration `0074_homeowner_profiles.sql`, lane number 0210)
Two tenant tables, both FORCE RLS with Layer 1 as written, the support_read_only policies and statement trigger, a SELECT policy and a system-only write policy:
- `homeowner_profiles`: one row per subject address (`UNIQUE (tenant_id, contact_address_id)`), FKs to `contacts` and `contact_addresses` ON DELETE CASCADE. `county_key` (`sc-beaufort`), `status` (`matched`, `not_matched`, `not_available`, `ambiguous`, `failed`), `match_detail` (exactly when not matched: `address_mismatch`, `owner_name_mismatch`, `missing_data`), `recognized_by` (1-4 of `seller_intent`, `seller_inquiry`, `homeowner_tag`, `past_buyer`), `parcel_ref` and `source` (the provider patterns), `record_as_of`, `checked_at` (the last answered lookup; NULL exactly when `failed`), `last_failure_at` and `last_failure_code` (`unavailable`, `timeout`, `rejected`, `address_invalid`; set together), `address_updated_at` (the fence), `next_refresh_at`, `retention_until` (at most 366 days after the last answer or the first failure).
- `homeowner_profile_facts`: one row per kept value, `PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, profile_id, field)`, a closed `field` list (`last_transfer_date`, `last_transfer_price_cents`, `county_market_value_cents`, `assessed_value_cents`, `annual_tax_cents`, `tax_year`, `exemption_legal_residence`, `exemption_homestead`, `owner_mailing_address`), one typed column per field (`date_value`, `cents_value` ≥ 0, `int_value`, `bool_value`, `text_value` ≤ 300 characters without control characters), `source` NOT NULL and `as_of`. No balance, equity, estimate, score or confidence is expressible.
- Guards (invoker): `app.homeowner_profiles_guard` (the subject address belongs to the profile's contact; contact and address never change; a profile leaves `matched` only once its facts are gone) and `app.homeowner_facts_guard` (a fact only under a `matched` profile).
- RLS: `profiles_select` = `retention_until > now()` AND the contact's detail visibility (`app.contact_detail_row`); `facts_select` = a visible parent; `profiles_write`/`facts_write` = FOR ALL, the system actor tenant-wide. The client principal reads nothing (Layer 1 is staff only). `app_user` holds SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE; DELETE is reachable only through the system policy. No definer.
- One platform pack, `platform:public_records_outreach@2026.11.0` (`{"states":["SC"]}`), ships unreviewed and gates nothing (no outreach path exists).

### 6.x D9 CMA (S0 migration)

Owner decision D9 (2026-11-12): sellers and past clients get an automated value range and a CMA built from the team's own MLS sold data, never a third-party AVM. It is an estimate, never an appraisal; it honours the MLS licence pack, and nothing reaches a client before its platform pack is cleared (`platform:avm` for automated home-value notes, `platform:cma` for client CMAs and seller-update ranges). The S0 migration (`0061_cma_schema.sql`, after batch 8, before lane V1-A's) is the schema only: no API, web or core code. It is proven by `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_cma.sql` (run by `rls_smoke_cma.test.ts`, which also checks `geo_cell` against a reference copy of the cell function on a coordinate table and 2,000 random pairs), by the generated isolation sweep, the 0001 §22 self-check and the drift test. The rules R-CMA-1…10 are in §9 (the D9 lanes add them); this section names a rule only where S0 implements its database part: R-CMA-1 (the stored columns and their bounds), R-CMA-6 (who reads a stored CMA), R-CMA-7 (the `platform:cma` pack) and R-CMA-8 (stored CMAs and the guard).

| Table | Purpose | Key fields | Invariants | Access (RLS) | Field classes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cma_reports` | A stored CMA: the subject facts with their sources, the result, the method and dates, and the disclosures (R-CMA-8). A research CMA has no contact | contact_id (FK `contacts`, ON DELETE CASCADE), contact_address_id (FK (tenant, contact_id, contact_address_id) → `contact_addresses (tenant_id, contact_id, id)`, ON DELETE SET NULL (contact_address_id): the address is the contact's from either side, so it cannot be moved to another contact while a CMA names it; its target is the new UQ `contact_addresses_tenant_contact_id_uq`), listing_id (FK `listings`, ON DELETE SET NULL (listing_id), so a future MLS purge never fails on a subject listing, F9), status (`draft/shared/superseded`, default draft), purpose (`listing_price/buyer_offer/other`), inspection (`none/exterior/interior_and_exterior`, default none) and inspected_on, the five SoP 11-1 items a purpose of `other` records (intended_users, agent_interest, conflicts, value_definition, limiting_conditions; F7b, **VERIFY V5/V7**), method_version, valuation_date, mls_data_as_of, subject and result (jsonb objects), value/low/high cents, agent_notes, content_sha256, created_by_user_id and shared_by_user_id (FKs `memberships (tenant_id, user_id)`), shared_at, shared_message_id (FK `messages`, ON DELETE SET NULL (shared_message_id): the compliance sweep's link to a queued share email, F1; one CMA per email, UQ `cma_reports_share_message`), created_at, updated_at | PK (tenant, id); every FK composite (tenant first); value, low and high all set or all null, each ≥ 0, low ≤ value ≤ high; an address needs a contact (`cma_reports_address_needs_contact`); inspected_on only with an inspection; each SoP item ≤ 2,000 characters, notes ≤ 4,000; content_sha256 64 lower-case hex; shared ⇒ shared_at and shared_by (`cma_reports_shared_state`); **one open draft per (creator, contact, address)**, a null address included (UQ `cma_reports_one_open_draft` … NULLS NOT DISTINCT WHERE status = 'draft' AND contact_id IS NOT NULL; F15); indexes (tenant, contact, created_at DESC) WHERE contact_id IS NOT NULL and (tenant, creator, created_at DESC); one index per SET NULL link, so each foreign-key action finds its rows without scanning the tenant's CMAs (F9): UQ `cma_reports_share_message` (tenant, shared_message_id), `cma_reports_address_idx` (tenant, contact_address_id) and `cma_reports_listing_idx` (tenant, listing_id), each WHERE the link IS NOT NULL; trigger `cma_reports_guard` (below) | read: tenant-wide roles, the creator (an active member), or anyone who sees the contact's detail (R-CMA-6); insert: a user actor with an active membership as themselves, for a contact whose detail they see or for none; update: the creator (an active member) or a tenant-wide role (a Team Lead is not tenant-wide); no DELETE (no policy, no grant); support reads with a grant, never writes; a client principal reads nothing | subject, result, notes and the SoP items are PII (the subject's address and facts); erased with the contact |

| Changed | Change |
|---|---|
| `listings` | + the MLS sale and property facts (R-CMA-1, the storage part), written only by the MLS sync (the system actor), all nullable unless stated: close_price_cents, close_date, purchase_contract_date, original_list_price_cents, concessions_cents (each money column ≥ 0); sale_conditions (`text[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, a subset of `standard/short_sale/reo/auction/probate/other`; `{}` = not recorded); days_on_market (≥ 0); latitude_e6 / longitude_e6 (integer microdegrees within ±90 / ±180, set as a pair: `listings_coordinates_pair`); **geo_cell**, generated and stored: `((floor(latitude_e6 / 14493.0) + 6300) * 25000 + (floor(longitude_e6 / 14493.0) + 12500))::integer`, cells of about 1 mi of latitude, range 2,250,080…312,749,919 (int4), NULL without coordinates; the division is numeric on purpose (integer division truncates toward zero and would double the cells at 0), and core/listings `geoCell()` mirrors it (lane V1-A); year_built (1600–2100), lot_sqft (≥ 0), subdivision_name, **garage_spaces_x10 and stories_x10** (tenths, like baths_x10, so 1.5 stories and 2.5 garage spaces are kept), pool_private, size_source (the RESO field sqft was read from; never a total, unfinished or below-grade area field: `listings_size_source_not_total`, migration 0082, R-MLS-7), is_lease (default false), avm_display_allowed (InternetAutomatedValuationDisplayYN, default false: never defaulted to allowed), address_key, mls_normalize_version (default 1). Coordinates are integers and the cell an int4 because int4, int8, date and text comparisons are leakproof on PG16 (numeric_lt is not), so the comps predicates can be index conditions under FORCE RLS (the 0039 and 0042 headers; lane V1-A adds the indexes). The columns stay out of `listings_mls_shape`; the price-history trigger (it fires only on a changed list price) and the monotonic trigger are unchanged. 0001's policies alone would not keep a person off these columns (a team listing is its agent's to write, and `listings_update` admits the Team Lead and tenant-wide roles on an MLS row with a WITH CHECK of only source = 'team'), so the invoker trigger `listings_valuation_facts_guard` refuses, for anyone but the system actor, app_definer, app_retention and the table owner (migrations): any of these columns other than its default on insert or changed on update (`LISTING_VALUATION_FACTS_SYSTEM`; lane V1-A gives team rows the empty valuation facts), and relabelling an MLS row as a team listing, which would take the row and its avm_display_allowed out of every source = 'mls' read (`LISTING_MLS_ROW_SYSTEM_ONLY`). The other listing columns are written as before. |
| `integration_connections` | + mls_backfill_pending (default false) and mls_backfill_cursor: the MLS backfill state (F14); a cursor exists only while a backfill is pending (`integration_connections_backfill_cursor_pending`). Written by the system only (lane V1-A's sync): the invoker trigger `integration_connections_backfill_guard` refuses Owner/Admin a non-default value on insert or any change on update (`CONNECTION_BACKFILL_SYSTEM`), with the same exemptions as `listings_valuation_facts_guard` (the table owner covers lane V1-A's one-time migration that starts the backfill); the policies and the 0018 guard are unchanged. |
| `contact_addresses` | + UQ `contact_addresses_tenant_contact_id_uq` (tenant_id, contact_id, id), the target of `cma_reports`' address foreign key. No path re-parents an address today; one a CMA names now cannot be. |
| `jurisdiction_packs` | + `platform:cma` (published, version 2026.11.0, rules `{}`, unreviewed; notes "Client CMAs and seller-update value ranges built from the team's MLS sales (C6 extended, V4, V5). Engineering placeholder. VERIFY WITH COUNSEL."; F8). It gates client CMAs and seller-update value ranges, which D11 does not list; `platform:avm` keeps gating automated home-value notes. `app.platform_cleared('platform:cma')` is false until counsel review is recorded through the counsel-review command (it accepts any `platform:[a-z_]+` key). This is the pack part of R-CMA-7; the gate itself belongs to the D9 lanes. |
| `jurisdiction_packs` | + `platform:gbp` (module 53, 0079; published, version 2026.11.0, rules `{}`, unreviewed, counsel-only, not covered by owner attestation D11; notes name C15). Gates review storage, reply drafts and posts to a `gbp_location` (R-GBP-3) | — | — | unchanged | none |

**cma_reports RLS** (R-CMA-6, the database part). Layer 1 is 0001's staff-only `tenant_isolation` (RESTRICTIVE), with the RESTRICTIVE `support_read_only_{ins,upd,del}` policies and the 0016 statement trigger `support_read_only`. `cma_reports_select`: `(SELECT app.is_tenant_wide()) OR (created_by_user_id = (SELECT app.user_id()) AND (SELECT app.my_role()) IS NOT NULL) OR (contact_id IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM app.contact_detail_row(tenant_id, contact_id)))`, 0031's inlinable twin of `app.contact_detail_visible` (`apps/api/src/load/deal_visibility_equivalence.test.ts` proves it equal to the per-row form for every role, support and the client), so the creator keeps their CMA after the contact goes to a pond, a collaborator or the Team Lead reads it through the contact's detail, and a pond member who sees only the pond card does not. `cma_reports_insert`: `app.actor_kind() = 'user' AND app.my_role() IS NOT NULL AND created_by_user_id = app.user_id() AND (contact_id IS NULL OR app.contact_detail_visible(tenant_id, contact_id))`. `cma_reports_update`: USING and WITH CHECK `app.is_tenant_wide() OR (created_by_user_id = app.user_id() AND app.my_role() IS NOT NULL)`. The creator branches need an active membership as a user actor, like `contacts_select` (the brief's text had none): a suspended member, or a context with a user id and no actor kind, reads and writes nothing. No DELETE. app_user holds SELECT, INSERT and UPDATE. An ISA or TC who sees a contact's detail passes this RLS; the API refuses them (`cma.prepare`, R-CMA-6, lane V1-C).

**cma_reports_guard** (R-CMA-8; plain PL/pgSQL, invoker, no SET clause, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC):
- A CMA is born a draft with no share fields (`CMA_BORN_DRAFT`), and created_by_user_id never changes (`CMA_CREATOR_IMMUTABLE`).
- contact_id never changes (`CMA_CONTACT_IMMUTABLE`): a CMA stays with the contact it was prepared for, whose detail the insert policy checked. No update moves a CMA onto a contact the caller does not see (the update policy does not re-check it), and the guard refuses before the foreign key, so a move is no probe of whether a contact id exists. No lane moves a CMA (V1-C's PATCH has no contact; the one-tap path inserts a new draft; a merge moves nothing; erasure cascades).
- Status moves only draft → shared, draft → superseded and shared → superseded (`CMA_STATUS_TRANSITION`). draft → shared is one UPDATE by a user actor as themselves: shared_by_user_id = `app.user_id()` and shared_message_id set in the same UPDATE (`CMA_SHARE_AS_SELF`), and the database stamps shared_at with its own clock (a caller's value is replaced). The share message must be an outbound email the sharer can see: the check runs as the caller, so RLS hides another person's message, and an inbound message or another tenant's is refused (`CMA_SHARE_MESSAGE`); `cma_reports_share_message` keeps it to one CMA. The guard does not bind the email to the CMA's contact: V2-A's share takes a recipient (`recipientContactId`), and queueOutboundInTx never queues to a merged-away record, so a merged-away record's CMA is emailed to its survivor. The share fields change at no other time (`CMA_SHARE_FIELDS`).
- A shared or superseded row never changes (`CMA_NOT_DRAFT`): not its content (contact_id, subject, result, value/low/high, agent_notes, purpose, inspection, inspected_on, the five SoP items, method_version, valuation_date, mls_data_as_of, content_sha256), its share fields, created_at or id. The only other changes allowed are status (shared → superseded), updated_at, and listing_id, contact_address_id or shared_message_id becoming NULL **through their foreign-key action** (F9): while the action runs, which PostgreSQL does as the owner of `cma_reports` (a role no application path can assume), and only once the target row is gone. A direct UPDATE that nulls a link is refused, even when its target is hidden from the caller by RLS.
- A draft whose address is deleted stays open with its address nulled, unless its creator already holds an open no-address draft for the contact: then that same action supersedes it, since the collision in `cma_reports_one_open_draft` would refuse the address's deletion and the contact's erasure. (The check runs inside the foreign-key action, as the table owner, so no row is hidden from it.)
- contact_address_id must be an address of contact_id, on insert and whenever it changes (`CMA_ADDRESS_NOT_OF_CONTACT`); the address foreign key, which carries the contact, holds it from the address side.

**Erasure, merge, retention.** `app.purge_contact_internal` deletes the contact rows, and the foreign key's cascade removes their CMAs, shared ones included (R-DATA-7). A merge moves nothing: the API lists the merged-away records' CMAs on the survivor (also to their creator when contacts RLS hides the loser from them, and since 0077 to whoever sees the survivor in detail, its agent and Team Lead included; the person's records are read through 0075's definer tree, so a chain through a closed middle record is followed whole) and refuses their edits to everyone, tenant-wide roles included (`CONTACT_MERGED`). One creator keeps one open draft per home across the person: a merge supersedes an open draft on the loser, or on any record merged into the loser before, whose creator holds another open draft for the same home (address key) on the survivor's side of the person, or a newer one on another of those records; and a one-tap or prepare on the survivor supersedes the caller's own open drafts for that home on every merged-away record of the person, also past a middle record the caller can no longer see (batch 10 review and re-review ownership-data/F4; api/cma's merge step, run by the ownership engine as the tenant-wide actor, and its step after a one-tap or prepare, run as the tenant's system actor). A merged-away record's only open draft for a home stays open. Within one live record the rule is per address row, not per address key (F15: `cma_reports_one_open_draft` is per creator, contact and address, and a one-tap or prepare supersedes the caller's open drafts of that address row only): a one-tap draft of an address row and a prepare of an entered or listing subject at the same street (which names no address row) are two open drafts of one creator for that home, while the same prepare on a survivor supersedes a merged-away record's draft of it by key (b11 merge review F2; pre-existing, unchanged by the batch 11 merge; owner question whether one record should follow the key rule too). No retention is built (C13, **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). S0 adds no SECURITY DEFINER function and changes no existing policy, grant or function; on existing tables it adds the two column guards above (`listings`, `integration_connections`) and one unique index (`contact_addresses`).

## 7. Phase 2-3 RLS and the client principal

- **Two principals.** `app.principal_type()` is `staff` (Phase 1) or `client` (Phase 3 portal). A client context sets `app.tenant_id`, `app.principal_type = 'client'` and `app.portal_account_id` from the portal session resolver only (module 43); `app.user_id` and `app.actor_kind` stay empty. `app.portal_account_id()` returns NULL unless the principal is `client`, so a staff session that sets the GUC gains nothing (smoke). `my_role()`, `is_member()` and `is_tenant_wide()` are false for a client.
- **Layer 1.** Every table that existed before 0015, and all 15 new staff tables, keep `tenant_id = app.tenant_id() AND principal_type = 'staff'`. Exactly three tables (`portal_accounts`, `portal_grants`, `portal_items`) use `principal_type IN ('staff','client')` for reads, keep staff-only `WITH CHECK`, add RESTRICTIVE `client_read_only_{ins,upd,del}`, and their triggers refuse a client principal outright (`CLIENT_READ_ONLY`). 0015's self-check fails the migration if any other table admits a client or loses its staff-only Layer 1.
- **Client Layer 2.** Own active account → own unrevoked, unexpired grants of that account on a deal or listing its contact is still a party to (`app.portal_party_ok`, a boolean definer; removing a co-buyer hides everything at once) → unretracted items of those grants (each subquery runs under the client's own policies). Expiry compares with `now()`, so a lapse is immediate. A client sees no staff table, no view and no other tenant (generated sweep in the smoke file).
- **Binding.** A grant can only name a party's own deal or listing and never moves; an item copies only a source of its grant's own deal or listing and never moves; revocation and retraction are final (all actors, the tenant-wide sync job included). `portal.document_object` (module 43, `0023_p3_portal_auth.sql`) re-checks the item, its grant, the document's scope, `shared_to_portal` and `scan_status` at download.
- **Staff policies on portal tables** all require `principal_type = 'staff'` explicitly.
- **Not principals:** web-chat visitors and public page viewers. They never get a tenant GUC; their reads go through definers the owning modules add (`app.webchat_poll`, `app.public_listing_page`), each a security-review item.
- **Definers added by 0015:** `app.ad_allocation_cap_check()` (deferred constraint trigger), `app.contacts_dnc_follow_merge()` (trigger), `app.portal_party_ok(uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid)` (boolean); all app_definer, pinned search_path. The two dispatch guards are replaced (same owner and search_path), and so are `app.purge_contact_internal` and `app.retention_sweep` (app_retention). `app_definer` gets column-level UPDATE on `contacts (do_not_contact_at, do_not_contact_event_id)` only. Planned later (each in its module's migration, each a review item): `app.resolve_channel_account`, `app.resolve_webchat_widget`, `app.webchat_session`, `app.webchat_poll`, `app.public_listing_page`, `app.record_person_opt_out` / `app.reinstate_person_contact` / `app.undo_language_opt_out`, `portal.request_login`, `portal.consume_login`, `portal.resolve_session`, `portal.document_object`, `app.coaching_trends`.
- **Definers added by the D2 migration** (both app_definer, pinned search_path, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, each a security-review item): `app.mail_copy_live(tenant, copy)` (boolean; takes only the copy's own row identity and looks the row up itself, S-D2-4: the context tenant, the copy's link live, its thread not excluded, its contact point current and confirmed in `mail_sync_points`, its address no live link's mailbox; granted to `app_user` for the RESTRICTIVE read policy only; since the D2 review fixes it answers only a caller the `mail_messages` read policies admit for that row: a staff principal, a user or system actor, and the system, the link's own user or a reader of the contact's timeline, tested through its own copy of `app.contact_detail_visible` because a definer reads `contacts` without the caller's RLS; false for a support actor, a client principal, no actor and a peer who holds the copy's id) and the trigger function `app.mail_sync_points_guard()` (no grant; it checks a confirmation against every live link's mailbox HMAC and a deal-only contact's points, which the confirming user cannot read, and returns at once unless the context tenant is set and is the row's). The draft's `app.mail_sync_live(tenant, link, thread)` is not created: it answered for any link and thread by id, an oracle for any member (S-D2-4). `app.mail_link_live(tenant, link)` and `app.mail_contact_related(tenant, user, contact)` are invoker functions (they reveal nothing the caller's own RLS does not show); the `mail_messages` insert guard is an invoker trigger, because only the system actor passes it and the system sees every link, exclusion and confirmation.
- **D2 review fixes** (one migration after the D2 one; post-merge review of the D2 schema): it replaces two definers with the same owner, search_path, volatility and grants, `app.resolve_channel_account` (0024's body without `ads_meta`: D2 has no Meta ads, so no Meta kind routes) and `app.mail_copy_live` (above); adds the invoker trigger `app.messages_rfc_guard` (R-GMAIL-12); adds the source `wrong_number` to `consent_events_synced_gmail` (R-GMAIL-9); reorders `mail_messages_select` (row-identical); and appends the rollout precondition to the `platform:gmail` notes. Proof: `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_gmail_review.sql`.
- **Gmail visibility model (D2).** `mail_messages` is read exactly like the contact's Phase 1 timeline (`app.contact_detail_visible`) plus the mailbox owner for their own link's rows; RESTRICTIVE `mail_messages_live`: only while `app.mail_copy_live` holds, so a disconnect, a deactivated member, the switch off, the pack uncleared, an exclusion, a removed contact point or a withdrawn confirmation hides the copies at once (the system still sees them, to delete them; S-D2-2); RESTRICTIVE `mail_messages_no_support`: never a support actor, even under an Owner support grant (**VERIFY** Google API Services User Data Policy); Layer 1 staff only, so never a client principal. `user_mail_links`, `mail_thread_exclusions` and `mail_sync_points` are self and system only. Team visibility stays the timeline audience (the task requirement; owner question Q-GMAIL-5, S-D2-8 a), and the connect disclosure says so. No database barrier keeps synced mail from an AI model (one login role; a GUC the caller sets is no barrier against the code it guards, S-D2-8 b): the enforcement is module 68's architecture test (no SQL names `mail_messages` outside `mailSync/**`; `mailSync/**` imports no model provider).
- **Photo and video texts (D5).**
  - `outbound_media_uploads` keeps the staff Layer 1. It is read and deleted by the uploader or the system, and inserted only by the uploader for a person they see in detail. There is no UPDATE.
  - `media_outbound_sender_insert` lets a message's sender attach to their own queued or scheduled `mms`. `app.message_media_outbound_guard` re-checks every insert, a tenant-wide actor's included.
  - The definer `app.outbound_media_upload_shred` (owner app_retention, pinned search_path, no PUBLIC EXECUTE) shreds only the deleted upload's own unattached `mms` object.
- **Homeowner profiles (D10, migration `0074_homeowner_profiles`; §6.z, R-HOME-7, R-HOME-9).** `homeowner_profiles` and `homeowner_profile_facts` keep the staff Layer 1 (a client principal reads nothing, E583) with the `support_read_only_*` policies and statement trigger (support reads under an Owner's grant and writes nothing). `profiles_select`: `retention_until > now()` AND `app.contact_detail_row` (exactly the contact's detail viewers; an expired row is hidden at once, E587); `facts_select`: a visible parent profile. `profiles_write` / `facts_write`: FOR ALL, the system actor tenant-wide, so every user role is refused INSERT and changes 0 rows by UPDATE or DELETE; DELETE is reachable only through the system policy. No definer; the two guards are invoker functions. Proof: `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_homeowner.sql`.
- **Superuser sessions** (the migration owner, test fixtures) are maintenance: `assistant_actions_decision_guard` does not bind them; no application path runs as a superuser (ARCHITECTURE §16). Every other 0015 guard also runs when no tenant context is set (`app.tenant_id() IS NOT NULL AND …` early return, as Phase 1's), so a definer or pre-tenant path cannot skip it.

| Mark a document shareable to the portal (`document.share`; clean deal or listing documents only; the database re-checks; a listing's Team Lead follows `listing_writable`) | ✅ | ✅ | T | own deals/listings | — | — | — |
| See a deal's or listing's portal state, preview a grant or an item (`portal.view`) | ✅ | ✅ | T | own deals/listings, deals they are on | — | deal | — |
| Invite to portal / grant / publish / retract / revoke / disable / change the login point (`portal.manage`) | ✅ | ✅ | T | own deals/listings | — | — | — |
| See own timeline, documents, showings, feedback, next steps | — | — | — | — | — | — | own grants |

TCs do not publish to the portal: confirmed milestones are rule-published by `portal.sync` (system), and a TC who needs another item asks the deal's agent (aligned with RLS: publishing needs deal write access).

## 9. Phase 2-3 business rules

Notation as §4. **smoke** here means `rls_smoke_p23.sql`.

### Tenancy, integrations, channels
- **R-TEN-11** Every new webhook resolves its tenant only through an enumerated definer that returns `(tenant_id, connection_id)` for a **connected or degraded** connection (a `pending_review` row reserves the account but routes nothing); payload fields never choose the tenant; inbound for an account with no such connection is `unrouted` with a platform alert. [DB definer + api]
- **R-TEN-12** Web-chat visitors and public-page viewers are not principals: no tenant GUC, reads only through `app.webchat_poll` (that session's outbound messages) and `app.public_listing_page` (one published snapshot), each returning minimal columns. [DB definer + api]
- **R-TEN-13** Every table keeps the staff-only Layer 1 except the three portal tables; the migration self-check enforces the allowlist. [DB self-check]
- **R-TEN-14** `app.portal_account_id()` is NULL unless `principal_type = 'client'`; the portal resolver is the only code that sets a client context, and staff and client transaction helpers are separate (`withTenantTx` / `withPortalTx`, lint). [DB + api + lint]
- **R-INT-1** Every external service is an adapter interface with a deterministic fake; tests and dev never reach a real service; a real adapter refuses to start without its configuration. Platform-procured vendors (public records, voice stack, malware scanner, text extractor) are platform configuration, not tenant connections; so are the county public-records connectors (D10: the Beaufort County GIS connector on module 50's one `PublicRecordsProvider`, R-HOME-10). No AVM vendor is procured: home values and CMAs are computed in-house from the tenant's own MLS sold data (D9, core/valuation). [api + architecture test]
- **R-INT-2** The UI offers only capabilities the adapter reports (`capabilities` is written only by the system), states the connection's status in words (e.g. "needs partner access"), and a connection that is not `connected`/`degraded` sends, publishes and polls nothing. [api + web + DB trigger]
- **R-INT-3** An external account (Page, IG account, MLS feed, ad account, GBP location, partner feed) is live in at most one tenant (global unique index over `pending_review/connected/degraded`). Only the provider callback (the system actor, after the adapter proved the token manages that account) links `external_account_id` or `credential_id` or moves an external kind into a live status; a live external kind needs both; the account id never changes (a different account is a new row). Owner/Admin create unconfigured rows, rename, set sources, widget origins and lead-email sender domains, edit the MLS licence pack and disconnect. Web-chat widgets and lead inboxes have no external account (their keys are server-generated) and may be switched on by Owner/Admin. [DB trigger, smoke]
- **R-INT-4** Adapters take explicit ids and credentials decrypted only by the system actor. [api]
- **R-INT-5** Disconnecting, or removing the member who owns a calendar link, revokes the grant at the provider (best effort) and locally in the same transaction; tokens are purged within 30 days (calendar links: by `calendar.reconcile`, hourly, which revokes the grant and then purges the token the 0049 trigger kept; `app.retention_sweep` does not cover `user_calendar_links`). **Suspension is not removal, for calendar and Gmail links alike** (b7 review): while the membership is `suspended` (or `deactivating`, or `invited`) the link is kept with its grant, it is hidden and its sync is paused (no push, no Gmail pass; Gmail copies are hidden by `app.mail_link_live`, never deleted); only `removed` revokes. On reactivation sync resumes by itself: Gmail at its next pass; for calendars, `calendar.reconcile` (hourly) enqueues every change the active member's connected calendar has not accepted yet (pushes that paused as `member_inactive`). A suspended member cannot sign in, so they never see their link while suspended; the link's status sentence is "Paused while your account is suspended." wherever it is rendered, and after reactivation the page shows it connected again, never revoked. The calendar page names a link in `pending_review` as its own waiting state ("Waiting for approval", never "Connected"), and while the deployment runs the fake adapters it says "This deployment uses a test calendar: nothing reaches your real calendar." and "Connected (test)" (batch 7 review ui/F3). [api + retention]
- **R-INT-6** A capability that waits on counsel review or a vendor check is gated on its published platform pack (`jurisdiction_packs` key `platform:<name>`) having `reviewed_by_counsel_at` set (and, for voice, `rules.vendor_ready_at`): Messenger/Instagram sending (`platform:meta`, DB guard), voice answering (`platform:voice`, DB at the toggle and at every AI call), publishing marketing (`platform:fair_housing`, DB), turning call scoring on (`platform:coaching`, DB), home-value notes (`platform:avm`, core; in-house notes also `platform:cma`, R-CMA-10), review requests (`platform:review_requests`, core), portal sign-in (`platform:portal`, module 43's definer), Gmail inbox sync (`platform:gmail`, which also needs `rules.vendor_ready_at`; DB at the Owner switch, the link and every synced row), Google Business Profile review storage, reply drafts and posts (`platform:gbp`, module 53, counsel-only, not covered by D11; DB guards and the publication gate; posts and replies also need `platform:fair_housing`), client CMAs, seller-update value ranges and in-house home-value notes (`platform:cma`, D9; `platform:avm` keeps gating automated home-value notes, and in-house notes need both, R-CMA-10; the gate is in the D9 lanes, the pack in §6.x; V1 built none of it, so its line said "not built in this version" (batch 10 review api-ui/F3); batch 13 builds all three (V2-A, V2-B), so the page names the pack "Client CMAs, seller value ranges and in-house home-value notes", its line says "off, awaiting counsel review" or "cleared" like any built capability's, and the `platform:cma` and `platform:avm` lines each name the other pack with its review state: b13 audit V2B-1), Google Ads budget changes and pauses (`platform:ads`, which also needs `rules.vendor_ready_at`; DB at every new approval and planned row; module 52, R-ADS-7), the AI assistant's ISA texts and emails (`platform:ai_isa`, D12 PROPOSED, not covered by D11; the dispatch guard's `ai_isa_mandate` branch and every chain check, R-ISA-1, R-ISA-25, R-ISA-31; named "The assistant's ISA texts and emails" on the page), the assistant's recent-sales emails to sellers (`platform:ai_isa_seller_data`, together with `platform:cma`; DB at dispatch; built by ISA-3 in batch 13: since batch 14 round 2 the page names it "The assistant's recent-sales emails" and its line says "off, awaiting counsel review" until counsel review is recorded ("cleared" for the pack after), naming `platform:cma` and the MLS licence pack's permission beside it, as `platform:cma`'s line names `platform:avm`; batch 12 review ui-2's "not built" wording is gone) and outreach using public-record personal information (`platform:public_records_outreach`, D10: it gates nothing in this build, no outreach path exists; named "Outreach using public-record data (not built)"; batch 12 review docs-1). **D2:** Messenger/Instagram sending is out of scope and `platform:meta` is never cleared, so the DB guard branch always refuses. The packs ship unreviewed; the UI says so; a later unreviewed version turns the capability off again. Replaces "feature flags". [DB + core, smoke]
- **R-CHAN-1** A Messenger, Instagram or web-chat identity is a `contact_points` row whose HMAC covers `{platform}:{external_account_id}:{scoped_id}`; its opt-outs are address-keyed exactly like phones and emails (R-CONSENT-1 unchanged). D2: only web chat is built; the Messenger/Instagram address forms stay in the CHECK until the cleanup, which retires this rule's Messenger/Instagram half, and no product path writes them. [DB + core]
- **R-CHAN-2** A person-level do-not-contact (`contacts.do_not_contact_at`, citing a revoke in the consent ledger) makes both dispatch guards refuse every message and call to that contact on every channel, including points added later, for every initiator (only the Phase 1 STOP/HELP reply to the number that sent the keyword is exempt); nobody on the team can clear it. A merge never loses it: the survivor takes the earliest one (definer trigger, along merge chains), and the guards also refuse a recipient a do-not-contact duplicate was merged into. A merged-away record is the whole person (F1, `0043_merged_person_opt_outs` `app.contact_person_ids`): canContact and both guards judge a recipient by every record of its person (the root survivor reached through `merged_into_contact_id` and every record merged into it), so the survivor's do-not-contact, and an opt-out on a phone only the survivor had, block a send addressed to the merged-away record exactly as one addressed to the survivor; and nothing new is addressed to a merged-away record (a text, email, call or enrollment of it is refused 409 `CONTACT_MERGED`, its saved searches send no alert). It is said before the tap wherever a send, call or chat reply is offered (batch 6 review): the reachability summary carries `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT` on every channel and point, the website chat included, also a point with no revoke of its own (a survivor's own phone, a point added later, the chat after "stop texting me"), in its own words ("Asked not to be contacted at all"), and the feed's channel hint denies every channel, so no Text, Email, Call or chat Reply is offered and nothing invites adding what the person typed in a chat. [DB, smoke]
- **R-CHAN-3** Opt-out language on any channel ("stop contacting me"), and an agent-recorded "do not contact me", set the person-level do-not-contact and revoke every channel on every point of the contact (`needs_review` for language). From a web-chat session this is always the session's own contact (a chat session is never attached to an existing contact by what the visitor typed, R-INBOX-1), so an anonymous visitor can never silence someone else. "Not an opt-out" (audited, reason required) reverses only what that language event wrote, through the reviewed definer. Closing a lead with reason `do_not_contact` records the same person-level opt-out in the same transaction (module 22). This tightens R-CONSENT-8 and never loosens it. [core + db]
- **R-CHAN-4** An exact keyword (STOP …) on Messenger or Instagram revokes that channel on every point of that kind on the contact and cancels queued messages on it; at most one confirmation goes to the sending identity within 24 h. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (keyword list per channel). D2: no Messenger/Instagram inbound exists, so no product path receives such a keyword; the 0018 DB guard branch and its smoke check stay until the cleanup, which retires this rule. [core + DB guard, smoke]
- **R-CHAN-5** `core-compliance/3.0.0` `canContact` blocks at least what the DB guard blocks and adds `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`, `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED` and `PLATFORM_NOT_CLEARED` (operational, not consent: D1 stands); `messenger` and `instagram` are always denied `PLATFORM_NOT_CLEARED`; the determinism and monotonic-denial property tests cover every new input; one shared case table (`packages/db/test/fixtures/opt_out_cases.json`) drives both the DB guard test and the core gate test. D2 history: the platform-window input and `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` were dropped with Meta DMs. [core + db test]
- **R-GATE-13** `messages_dispatch_guard` maps every channel explicitly and raises `CHANNEL_UNKNOWN` for anything else (no `ELSE 'sms'` fallthrough); adding a channel changes the `messages.channel` CHECK, the guard map and core's `Channel` union together (architecture test: the three lists are equal). D2: core's `Channel` union keeps `messenger`/`instagram` only to equal the 0018 CHECK; both are always denied. [DB + architecture test]
- **R-GATE-14** Every new outbound path (DM and chat replies (D2: web chat only), open-house invites, seller updates, milestone updates, home-value notes, review requests, portal login links and notices, voice booking confirmations, CMA shares (module 71)) goes through `dispatchOutbound` and has a path test showing a blocked send writes `gate_decisions` and reaches no provider. [api + DB]
- **R-GATE-15** The reserved outbound method names `deliverChannelMessage` (`comms/dispatch.ts`, web chat), `publishPost` (Facebook Pages, Instagram business accounts and GBP locations, R-MKT-12), `postReviewReply` (`marketing/publish.ts`, called only inside its `sendReviewReply` wrapper, which only `gbp/replies.ts` imports) and `applyBudgetChange` and `pauseCampaign` (`ads/dispatch.ts`) are callable only from their single path module; no interface has a method that places an AI outbound call. D2 history: `sendDirectMessage` was dropped with Meta DMs. [lint + architecture test]

### Unified inbox
- **R-INBOX-1** Every channel's messages live in the contact's one conversation; an identity with no exact match creates a contact through intake routing with the connection's lead source, never by name match (a name match only suggests a duplicate; merge is R-OWN-24). A phone or email typed into web chat is an unverified claim (`webchat_sessions.claimed_*`): it never attaches the session to an existing contact and never becomes a contact point by itself; a match only raises a duplicate suggestion that says "typed in chat, not verified". A merge (a human decision, R-OWN-24) moves the person's chat sessions, and so their claims, to the survivor: the survivor answers the open chat, and a returning visitor's claim matches the survivor. [core + db]
- **R-INBOX-2** Inbound on every new channel runs the Phase 1 ingestion transaction: pause every enrollment on every contact sharing the identity, cancel queued assistant messages **and scheduled trigger runs of those contacts** (module 22 cancels them by contact; module 26's `executeTriggerRun` re-checks the pause), create exactly one handback. [db/api tests per channel]
- **R-INBOX-3** D2: the DM part is out of scope (the 0018 guard branch stays until the cleanup, which retires this rule, and always refuses `PLATFORM_NOT_CLEARED`); web chat is unchanged. The dispatch guard maps Messenger/Instagram/web chat to their own consent channel and point kind; an outbound DM must name its connection, and a DM to an identity that opted out (on that identity or any of the person's identities of that kind: every record of the person since 0043, and canContact reads the same set since the batch 6 review), is suppressed, or belongs to a person-level do-not-contact is refused for every initiator. [DB, smoke]
- **R-INBOX-4** D2: DMs are out of scope, so `inbound_channel` resolves to web chat only. The assistant uses DM and chat channels only for the 60-second first response and human-approved drafts. The first response's step channel is `inbound_channel` ("reply where the lead wrote"), part of the hash-bound content the Owner approved; `PlaybookStep.channel` is `sms | email | inbound_channel`; no multi-turn autonomous replies. A chat draft (an `inbound_channel` step held for approval) is shown and judged as a chat reply everywhere (batch 6 review): the feed says "Chat reply drafted for you" and offers Reply in the chat or Send, judged by the chat and the person (never by the texts' opt-outs), and the approvals inbox captions it "Chat reply the assistant drafted". A handback after a chat reply offers Reply in the chat instead of "Can't reach them right now"; its suggested reply is the assistant's text or email (the server binds it to sms or email, sms even when there is no number), so its one-tap Send is judged by that draft's own channel, never by the chat: no number on file, or a STOP on it, and the card offers no Send (Reply in the chat first, the draft kept to edit), and the web disables a Send of a text or email to a lead with no number or address, with the reason (review chat-R2 of batch 6). A chat the gate summary blocks (the person's do-not-contact, or an opt-out of the chat, worded from its source: "Marked as a wrong number", "Asked in the chat to stop being contacted") offers no Reply there, and the card says that reason: first in "Can't reach them right now" when nothing else works, else as "Can't reply in the chat: …" (CHAT_BLOCKED, unscored; review chat-R3 of batch 6). [core]
- **R-INBOX-5** A web-chat session creates no contact, offer or notification until a first message that passes the spam checks and carries a name plus a phone or email, after the visitor saw the disclosure; the visitor reads only that session's outbound messages; nothing automated is ever sent to the typed phone or email (an agent may add it to the contact by hand and call or text, a human decision shown as not verified); per-widget and per-IP rate limits (`auth.rate_limit_hit`) cap sessions and messages; sessions with no contact are purged after 30 days. The embed code an Admin copies carries only `data-widget-key`; the widget fetches the team name and the current disclosure from the server, so a changed notice never goes stale on a customer's website (batch 7 review ui/F4). [api + DB definer + retention]
- **R-INBOX-6** **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11) put Messenger/Instagram DMs out of scope; no platform-window input, `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` or window copy is built, and `platform_window_until` stays unused until the cleanup.** Old text: Platform messaging windows are data in the platform pack (`platform:meta`); the instant is stored on the point at inbound (`platform_window_until`); outside it every initiator is denied `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` and compose offers the contact's other allowed channels. **VERIFY** current Meta policy (window length, human-agent tag). [core + api]
- **R-INBOX-7** Disconnecting a channel stops its sends at once (`CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`); history, identities and opt-outs stay; reconnecting the same account in the same tenant restores identities by HMAC. [api]
- **R-INBOX-8** **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): the product receives no Meta webhooks (no DMs, no Lead Ads; the publishing connection subscribes to none).** Old text: Meta webhook signatures are verified over the raw body before parsing; a bad signature writes only the rejected webhook row. [api]
- **R-INBOX-9** Portal and ad leads (Zillow, realtor.com, Meta Lead Ads) enter only through lead intake (routing, attribution, dedup). D2: Meta Lead Ads intake is removed; Zillow and Realtor.com stay, through partner feeds where access exists (`zillow_feed`, `realtor_feed`, `needs_partner_access` otherwise) and DKIM-aligned lead-email parsing. A lead email counts as a source lead only with a DKIM pass aligned to one of the connection's `allowed_sender_domains`; anything else is `needs_review` with no autonomous first response. A parsed lead email shows each extracted field with its source span; a field not found stays empty; no phone and no email ⇒ an Admin review card and no routing. [core + api]
- **R-LEM-1** Each lead source has at most one lead-email inbox (a keyed `lead_email` connection). Its address `leads+<key>@<inbound domain>` carries a server-generated 160-bit key; only sha256(key) routes; the key is stored envelope-encrypted so Owner/Admin can see and copy the address again; only Owner/Admin see it, and turning it on is 🔒 and audited (`lead_email.inbox_enabled`). [DB + api]
- **R-LEM-2** The key alone resolves tenant, inbox and lead source, through `app.resolve_lead_email_key` (never `app.resolve_channel_account`): only the current key of a connected/degraded inbox of an active lead source in an active tenant. Rotation (🔒, audited `lead_email.key_rotated`) retires the old key in the same transaction; a retired, guessed, malformed or other-domain address is unrouted and writes no tenant row. [DB + api]
- **R-LEM-3** Trust: a lead email creates a lead automatically ONLY when the receiving provider reports a DKIM pass whose signing domain and the From domain both sit under one of the inbox's `allowed_sender_domains` (relaxed alignment; the allowlist entry is the organizational domain). DKIM-Signature or Authentication-Results headers written by the sender are never evidence (Postmark: only the topmost Authentication-Results with the configured authserv-id). Anything else is held (`DKIM_MISSING`, `DKIM_FAILED`, `DKIM_UNALIGNED`, `SENDER_NOT_ALLOWED`, `FROM_INVALID`). [api]
- **R-LEM-4** A trusted email with a parsed, valid phone or email goes through the existing intake in the same transaction: dedupe by address, an inquiry with the inbox's lead source (`parse_status 'parsed'`, spans), routing through the ownership engine, and the 60-second first response to the LEAD's own phone or email; the portal's From address never becomes a contact point; opted-out addresses are never auto-messaged (D1, decided at send time). [api]
- **R-LEM-5** Held email (also `UNKNOWN_FORMAT`, `PARTIAL_FORMAT`, `NO_CONTACT_POINT`) creates no contact, no routing, no job and sends nothing; its subject, text and parse are stored encrypted with the reason, with the tenant-HMACs of the addresses it names (`point_hmacs`, keys only). The only transitions are `needs_review → accepted | discarded`, once, by a person (DB trigger), and, by the retention role only, `needs_review → erased` (the person it names was erased, R-DATA-4) or `→ expired` (30 days after receipt, R-DATA-3): both keep nothing of the person but the message key (migration 0041). An erased or expired email is not listed, accepted or discarded (`NOT_FOUND`). [DB + api]
- **R-LEM-6** Owner/Admin/Team Lead see the Needs review list with the reason in words; accepting runs the same intake (audited `lead_email.review_accepted`, the reviewer may type a missing phone/email/name) WITHOUT an autonomous first response (R-INBOX-9); discarding is audited (`lead_email.review_discarded`); both purge the stored content; a second decision is `LEAD_EMAIL_NOT_PENDING`; accepting with no contact point is `LEAD_EMAIL_NO_CONTACT_POINT`. [api]
- **R-LEM-7** An allowlisted sender domain is a registrable domain: no wildcard, no scheme, no bare public suffix (`com`, `co.uk`, …); lookalikes (`evilzillow.com`, `zillow.com.evil.test`) never match `zillow.com`. Seed suggestions `zillow.com`, `realtor.com` are **VERIFY**; the Owner sees each labelled by the portal's name and marked "suggested", with one sentence that they are unconfirmed until a test lead from each portal becomes a lead automatically (arriving is not enough: a test lead held for review confirms nothing; never the VERIFY marker). Changes are 🔒 and audited. Consumer mailbox domains are never allowed (R-LEM-11, `LEAD_EMAIL_DOMAIN_INVALID` with reason `consumer_mailbox`). The suggestions are a per-portal catalog (R-LEM-9): Zillow and Realtor.com pre-ticked, the others ticked only when the Owner picks that portal. [api]
- **R-LEM-8** The inbound webhook verifies the provider's signature (generic: X-Realty-Signature; Postmark/SendGrid: Basic), caps the body (1 MB JSON, 5 MB multipart) before parsing, drops attachments unread, strips HTML in linear time, never fetches links or images, and is replay-safe: webhook_events per inbox + message id, then the per-tenant UNIQUE message key in the lead's transaction (holds after the webhook_events purge); a sender-chosen Message-ID can never pre-empt another inbox. Parsed values carry no CR/LF, controls or bidi overrides. [api]
- **R-LEM-9** More portals' lead emails are read through the generic labelled format and leadmetadata, until real samples exist. Sender domains are offered per portal (Zillow, Realtor.com pre-ticked; Redfin, Homes.com/Homesnap, Trulia and Opcity on request; all **VERIFY**). Their domains are portal domains, whose addresses are never a lead's. Vendor rows for those portals wait for real sample emails and counsel's answer on the US 10,783,530 family. An email nothing reads is held (E78), and the page asks for a sample. [api + web]
- **R-LEM-10** leadmetadata.org v1.0 is read when an email's raw HTML declares it. Only real `lead_*` meta elements are read (head or body), never text made by entity decoding, a text/plain part, or comments, script, style, textarea or title. They are read in linear time, capped (64 tags, 2,000 characters a value) and cleaned, then prepended to the normalized body as labelled lines, so every field cites a span (R-INBOX-9). A name given twice with different values holds the email (`PARTIAL_FORMAT`). A tag is read only when its attribute region is clean (plain attribute names; `name` and `content` once each; no `<`, quote or entity-escaped angle bracket in an unquoted value): a tag that is not clean never declares and gives no value, and the name it claims counts as given twice, so the email is held. `lead_source` never changes the source. Other versions are not read. Trust is unchanged. [api]
- **R-LEM-11** Consumer mailbox domains are never allowed sender domains. The list is fixed and **VERIFY**. Saved entries are ignored and shown as not allowed. Mail from such a domain is held `PERSONAL_MAILBOX`, and only the forwarded block is read (no marker: nothing pre-filled). The parser keeps a lead's own address under a consumer domain. It never keeps the email's own From address, a member's account email or alert phone (any member who joined: active, suspended, deactivating or removed; b9 review ownership-data/F3, so a suspended forwarder's own signature never joins two leads), or one of the tenant's live numbers. It drops the portal's addresses and a non-consumer sender's own addresses. [api]
- **R-LEM-12** Automatic forwarding into a source address is supported. The key is read from every recipient, envelope included. It is trusted only through the original signature as the receiving provider reports it (**VERIFY** that the signature survives forwarding). Gmail's forwarding confirmation (from google.com, with an aligned google.com DKIM pass and the confirmation subject; **VERIFY**) is held `FORWARDING_CONFIRMATION` and says in words who asked. It is never a lead and cannot be accepted (Discard only, `LEAD_EMAIL_NOT_A_LEAD`; Needs review offers no Accept for it), and the source shows that one is waiting. [api + web]

### Lead intake: sources, forms, tracking numbers

- **R-LSRC-1** Owner/Admin set up lead sources on one page. Each source shows its kind and a status in words, from stored facts only: last lead received, leads in the last 30 days, held emails waiting, off since when, and per path On / Off / Not set up / Not available on this deployment / Needs partner access. Each path shows its setup steps. Website chat, partner feeds, typing a person in and imports are linked from the page, not rebuilt. Nothing is shown as working that the deployment cannot do: no inbound email domain, no public https address, test phone numbers, no partner access, live texting and calling off (the overview's `deployment.telephony`, `live`/`off`/`simulated`, also on GET /numbers: the tracking path, Add a source and Phone and email say "Live texting and calling are off on this deployment: numbers can be set up but nothing reaches a phone yet." before any tap, and buying a number is not offered while the kill switch is off). Website chat reads the same as on the Website chat page: a widget that page calls live (connected or degraded) is On, and "Not working: no consent text" while the source has none; each page's changes refresh the other's. Changes to a source's lead email (on, sender domains, a new address) refresh the overview at once, so the list never copies a dead address. [api + web]
- **R-LSRC-2** Renaming and the consent text are `settings.routing`, audited. A new consent text is a new `consent_disclosures` row. Earlier inquiries keep citing theirs (R-CONSENT-4; history only, D1). Website chats show the new text (R-INBOX-5). Turning a source off or on is 🔒 and audited, and records when. An off source takes nothing on any path, and what arrives is not kept: the intake token and the lead-email address are unrouted; the Google key is refused (Google does not resend; its leads stay downloadable in Google Ads for 30 days); partner-feed leads are skipped, each with a receipt (`lead_source_skips`, reason `SOURCE_OFF`, no lead content) that the page counts ("N leads skipped while this source was off"), and the feed cursor advances, so turning the source back on does not replay them; website chat stops; tracking numbers fall back to the unknown-caller path, so a call is never dropped. The off confirm says this in words, and the card says "Off since <time>". Sources are never deleted. [api + DB + web]
- **R-LSRC-3** A source's intake URL token and its own signing secret are server-generated, shown once and rotated together (🔒, audited `lead_source.webhook_rotated`). Only sha256(token) is stored. The secret is envelope-encrypted in a system-only table. A source with its own secret accepts only its own X-Realty-Signature. A source without one keeps the deployment secret. Old values stop working at once. Concurrent rotations are serialized by the source's credential lock. [DB + api]
- **R-LSRC-4** The routing preview is read-only. It uses the router's own match, candidate facts and selection (core `previewRouting` over the shared candidate query). It reads the rotation cursor but never locks or advances it. It writes and sends nothing. It names, for a lead arriving now with the given type, ZIP and price band: the rule, the person or pond, each skipped member's reason in words, and whether the 60-second reply would be queued. [core + db + api]
- **R-LSRC-5** Agent forwarding is a lead source, its lead-email inbox (with the chosen portals' sender domains) and a direct routing rule, made through the existing endpoints. No ownership path is added (R-OWN-10). The preview shows whether that rule wins. The steps put the address in the portal first, then automatic forwarding (Gmail filter; Outlook "Redirect to"). [web]
- **R-LSRC-6** Every channel creates or attaches a lead only through `ingestInquiryInTx`: lead email, intake webhook, Google lead form, tracked numbers, partner feeds and web chat. The untracked unknown-caller path keeps `create via inbound`. Gmail sync never creates leads (R-GMAIL-14). [architecture test]
- **R-LSRC-7** A provider delivery whose processing failed, or whose run crashed (older than the stale window), is processed again when the provider retries. A delivery that was processed, unrouted or rejected stays as it is. Every handler behind it is idempotent. [api + DB]
- **R-LSRC-8** **No automated contact for leads from this source** (b18 review B18S-5; owner product boundary 2026-11-14: a referral partner is not an AI cohort, and its first-week email introduction does not permit this CRM's autonomous texting, calling or mass enrolment; first built as "No AI care for leads from this source", b18 item f). The Owner-only lead-source setting (`lead_sources.no_ai_care`, 0094; the API field `noAiCare` and `PUT /lead-sources/:id/ai-care` keep their names) covers every person the source sent: any record of the person and any inquiry of theirs (`app.person_no_ai_care_source`, 0096), and anyone a Follow Up Boss or CSV import COMMITTED (a person it created, or a person already here onto whom it merged or attached a record) whose imported source label the Owner mapped to the source (b18 round 3 item 1, E728: `lead_sources.import_labels` and `app.person_from_lead_source`, 0099; since migration `0103_b18_source_committed_only` only a committed record counts, exactly as for R-LSRC-9: a dry run, a record that only matched a person or waits for review, a skipped, rejected or reverted record, an abandoned import and an `import_source` key a caller sends at intake cover nobody, E755; the Owner maps a label exactly as the import stored it, at import time from the import's preview or afterwards from the lead sources page, `PUT /lead-sources/import-labels`, Owner only in the API and the database; the import's preview and summary list the labels found and which are marked; the owner decided on 2026-11-14 that this review is the default step of every import and that an imported source nobody has reviewed disables automations: R-LSRC-9). For them nothing automatic goes out: no 60-second first response by text, email or chat (the lead card says why, and the source's routing preview says the instant reply is off); no plan enrolment of any kind, whether a stage entry (skip `source_no_automated_contact`), a closed deal's post-closing plan, a bulk enroll, or a person enrolling one lead by hand (`409 SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT`: the conservative default of owner question OQ-B18-S5); a running plan stops (`stop_reason source_no_automated_contact`, its unsent steps canceled) the next time its step job runs, due or not: the step runner checks the source before its due check (b18 round 3 item 4), so the step that turning the setting on, mapping a label or the source's new inquiry asks for stops it at once and not when its next step comes due and a paused one cannot be resumed (`POST /enrollments/:id/resume` answers `409 SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT`; "Resume automation", `POST /contacts/:id/automation/resume`, lifts only the conversation pause a reply set, leaves the paused plan as it is and answers `notResumed` with the count, the code and the sentence "1 plan was not resumed: …": b18 round 3 item 2, never a 500); no module 26 trigger message (skipped `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT` with its sentence); no saved-search alert or other assistant or system message; no bulk text or email and no open-house invite (each leaves them out and counts them: "N came from a lead source marked …", "Sent 2 of 3; 1 left out (No automated contact for leads from their source)"); no AI care (start, Continue and resume refused `AI_ISA_SOURCE_NO_AI_CARE`, chain link 11, R-ISA-1; a handoff's pending appointment confirmation or holding line, which belongs to an assignment that has ended and is judged on links 1 to 3 only, is refused `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT` by dispatch TX1 itself and by the database guard, b18 round 3 item 5, and the handoff card then asks for a hand confirmation); and no AI-answered call (the takeover is refused `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT`, Phase 1's voicemail). A draft a plan step or a module 26 trigger wrote for a person to approve (`assistant_actions.trigger_ref.kind` `playbook_step` or `trigger_run`) stays automated even when a person taps it (b18 round 3 item 3; the conservative default of owner question OQ-B18-S6): the pending ones are withdrawn (`status invalidated`, `payload.withdrawn { reason: SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT, at }`), their feed cards close and a trigger run that waited on one is skipped with the setting's sentence, when the setting is turned on or a label is mapped to the marked source (the Owner's answer counts them: `withdrawn`, "1 draft a plan or an alert wrote for them was withdrawn."), when the marked source sends the person again (at intake), and when a plan step, a trigger firing or the first response meets such a person; approving one is refused `409 SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT` with `details.sentence` ("This draft was written by a plan or an alert, and "No automated contact for leads from this source" is on for this person's lead source (an Owner setting), so it was withdrawn and nothing was sent. Call, text or email them one to one."), never `DRAFT_INVALIDATED`'s "the lead's owner changed"; one approved before the mark whose message is still queued is blocked at dispatch, and 0100 widens `app.messages_source_guard` to refuse it in the database. A reply the assistant drafted to the person's own message or call, a handback reply and a seller update are a person's one-to-one sends and are not withdrawn. A person's own one-to-one call, text, email and chat reply still go, and the keyword replies the person asked for (STOP confirmation, HELP) and a client's own portal sign-in link are answers, not outreach. Dispatch TX1 refuses any automated or bulk message still queued for them (`SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT`, "Not sent: their lead source is marked …"), and 0098's invoker guards refuse the same in the database: a live enrolment (`app.playbook_enrollments_source_guard`), an automated message moving to dispatching (`app.messages_source_guard`) and an AI-answered call (`app.calls_source_guard`). Turning the setting on also asks each of the source's live plans for a step now, which stops it at once, and the Owner's answer counts the AI care pausing (`pausing`, by lead), the plans stopping (`stopping`, by plan) and the drafts withdrawn (`withdrawn`), each for this source's own people only (`app.person_from_lead_source`), never another marked source's, and found from the source's side (its own records, its inquiries and the committed people of each label and no-label group it holds: `leads/sourceStop.ts`, scope `{ sourceId }`), never by asking about every plan, AI care and draft of the team (E757) (round 3 item 4; the page reads "AI care already running for 2 of its leads will pause. 1 plan running for its leads will stop."). Automated messages are never human contact (R-OWN-14) and attempts are not conversations (R-ACC-4). [DB + api + web]
- **R-LSRC-9** **An imported person whose source is not reviewed gets no automated contact** (owner decision 2026-11-14; migration `0101_b18_source_unclassified`; the enforcement half, the review step and its list follow). **What unclassified means.** A person is unclassified while at least one COMMITTED contacts import record of theirs (`import_records.outcome` `imported` or `merged`, targeting any record of the person: a merged record counts through its survivor) carries a source label that the Owner has not mapped: no lead source holds it (`lead_sources.import_labels`), or more than one does (ambiguous); or carries no label at all and the Owner has not classified that import's no-label group (`lead_sources.import_unlabeled_batches`, one group per import, never across imports: a missing label in one file says nothing about another). The label is compared whole, exactly as imported; nothing ever infers that one label means another. A dry run, a rejected, skipped, duplicate-only or reverted record, a record that only matched a person, and any key a caller sends at intake are not import evidence, for this reason and, since migration 0103, for the marked-source reason too (both rest on the same committed records, E755): a preview changes nothing for anyone, and a native lead (web form, email, call, chat, API intake) is never unclassified. **Enforcement rides the same check.** Every automated path, dispatch TX1 and the database guards already ask one definer (`app.person_no_ai_care_source`); it is now true for either reason, and `app.person_source_refusal` says which: `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT` (a marked source sent them: R-LSRC-8; it wins when both apply) or `SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED`. So an unclassified person is refused exactly where a marked source's person is: the 60-second first response; a plan by hand, a resume, a stage entry (skip `source_unclassified`), a closed deal's plan, a bulk enroll or send (counted `SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED`); a module 26 trigger (skipped with its sentence); an open-house invite (outcome `source_unclassified`, counted on its own); a saved-search alert and any other automated message at dispatch (a recorded deny); AI care start, Continue, resume and chain link 11 (`AI_ISA_SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED`, pause reason `source_unclassified`); an AI-answered call; the approval of a draft a plan step or an alert wrote (409, the draft withdrawn); and the database guards on enrolments, messages (on INSERT in a sending state as well as the move to dispatching), calls and AI care. No path can have one check and miss the other. **The reason is told truthfully.** `SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED` has its own sentence everywhere a refusal is explained (the API's sentences in `leads/automatedContact.ts` and `isa/sentences.ts`, and the web's word tables): the person came in from an import, their imported source has not been reviewed yet, and an Owner reviews it under Lead sources. It never says the Owner turned a setting on. A person's own one-to-one call, text and email still go; the STOP confirmation, the HELP reply and a client's own portal sign-in link keep exactly the exemptions of R-LSRC-8, no wider. **Becoming unclassified stops things at once, from the API.** When an import commits a record onto a person already here, or when the Owner removes a mapping, the request's own transaction asks each running plan of the affected people for a step (the step runner stops it, `stop_reason source_unclassified`, due or not), each live AI care for a turn (its chain pauses it) and withdraws their pending plan and alert drafts with the reason; the audit row counts what was asked. A label held by two sources is different: no API path can make one (the Owner's mapping always moves a label off the source that held it, `leads/sources.ts` `setImportSource`), so no request stops anything for it. If a direct database write makes one, the definer treats the label as unclassified (as the marked source's when one of the two is marked) and its people are refused on every path from then on, but nothing is stopped at once beyond those refusals: a running plan stops at its next step, live AI care pauses at its next turn and a pending draft is refused when someone approves it. The work is bounded (one label's or one chunk's people, through an index; never a tenant scan) and takes no tenant-wide or advisory lock: an opt-out, an inbound message or call, a pause, a stop and a person's one-to-one send never wait on, or fail because of, a mapping or an import. A stopped plan is not restarted and a withdrawn draft is not restored when the Owner later reviews the source. **The Owner's mapping** (`PUT /lead-sources/import-labels`, Owner only in the API and the database, re-authenticated every time): `{ label, leadSourceId | null }` for a label, `{ unlabeledImportId, leadSourceId | null }` for one import's no-label group. It can be changed and removed; after every change the list tells the truth (`inReview`, the sentence "not reviewed yet") and the people follow it in both directions: mapped to an unmarked source the refusal lifts; mapped to a marked source they are refused for that reason; removed they are unclassified again. Nobody is blocked with no way out. **Existing data.** Migration 0101 changes no plan, message, draft or import row: from the moment it is applied every already-imported person whose label is not already Owner-mapped reads as unclassified and is refused; what is running stops when its next step, turn or dispatch runs, or when the Owner next acts. **What a race can still send** (no lock, stated plainly): the one message whose dispatch TX1 committed before a mapping removal or an import commit did; the next step and every later send are refused. Tests: `packages/db/test/source_unclassified_0101.test.ts`, `source_unclassified_upgrade_0101.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/leads/sourceUnclassified.test.ts`, `.stop.test.ts`, `.guards.test.ts`, `.races.test.ts`, and the unclassified cases of `nurture/`, `marketing/`, `voice/noAutomatedContact.test.ts`, `isa/sourceNoAiCare.test.ts` and `imports/noAutomatedContact.test.ts`. Edge cases E729 to E737. Security notes: ARCHITECTURE §21.68.
- **R-LSRC-10** **Every import shows a source review, and the Owner's mapping is recorded** (owner decision 2026-11-14, the review half; migration `0102_b18_import_source_review`; built on R-LSRC-9, whose enforcement it does not change). **The list.** One row per imported source label per team and one row per import's no-label group (`import_source_labels`). The label is kept EXACTLY as imported: core `sourceLabel` returns the string the file or Follow Up Boss gave, never trimmed, re-cased, normalized or cut (two labels that differ in any character are two labels; nothing or only whitespace is no label; a label that cannot be stored exactly, one holding U+0000 or half of a surrogate pair or longer than 32,768 characters, rejects its record `SOURCE_LABEL_UNREADABLE` rather than being altered). The same string is on the import record, on the person an import creates, in `lead_sources.import_labels` and on every row. **Each row says**: its `state`: `mapped` (to the named lead source), `suggested` (an exact known match, with the suggested source), `ambiguous` (it reads the same as two lead sources, or two hold it) or `in_review`; how many people carry it (the CRM-wide list counts people an import COMMITTED with it, from the committed records themselves, so the count cannot go stale; an import's own rows count that import's people and how many of them it has committed; and an import's own row mapped to a marked source also counts, as `matchedOnlyPeople`, how many of them were already in the CRM and are not imported from this import, whom the mapping does not cover, and its sentence says the three numbers instead of "nothing automatic reaches the people imported with it", which only the CRM-wide list says, of the committed people it counts: E755); where it was first seen (the import that first committed a person with it, that import's kind `csv` / `fub_api` / `fub_export`, and when); and who last mapped, re-mapped or un-mapped it, and when. **Only committed outcomes are evidence.** The review records are written by the contacts commit, in its own chunk's transaction, and by the Owner's mapping; a dry run, a preview, a rejected, skipped or duplicate record, a record that only matched a person and an abandoned import write nothing and block nobody. (A label the Owner mapped ahead from a preview and then un-mapped, which no import ever committed, labels nobody and is not a row of the CRM-wide list.) The demo seed imports four people from a file and makes the Owner's review an explicit step: one suggested source confirmed, one label mapped by choosing, one label and the person with no label left in review (`apps/api/src/seed/demo.ts`, `demo.test.ts`). **Suggestions.** The only suggestion logic is core `suggestLeadSource`: an exact lead-source name or Owner-confirmed label, apart from capital letters and surrounding spaces; two sources matching is ambiguous. A suggestion is shown, never applied: the Owner confirms it (`POST /lead-sources/import-labels/confirm`), and the server maps only if the suggestion still names exactly that source (`409 IMPORT_LABEL_SUGGESTION_CHANGED` otherwise). Nothing infers that one label means another. **Routes.** `GET /lead-sources/import-labels` (who manages routing: Owner, Admin) is the CRM-wide list; `PUT /lead-sources/import-labels` `{ label | unlabeledImportId, leadSourceId | null }` maps, re-maps and un-maps (Owner only, password every time); the import's preview (`GET /imports/:id/parity`) and summary (`GET /imports/:id/report`) carry the same rows, states and counts for that import. The no-label group is one row, mapped like a label, and nothing is ever suggested for it. Every other role gets what the lead-source permissions gave it before, never more. **Record and audit.** Each mapping, re-mapping, un-mapping and confirmation writes, in the Owner's transaction, the review record (`lead_source_id`, `mapped_by_user_id`, `mapped_at`; the database lets only the Owner write these, as themselves and in their own name) and an audit row `routing.changed` (`LEAD_SOURCE_IMPORT_LABEL_MAPPED` / `_REMAPPED` / `_UNMAPPED` / `_SUGGESTION_CONFIRMED`, `LEAD_SOURCE_IMPORT_UNLABELED_MAPPED` / `_REMAPPED` / `_UNMAPPED`) with the label (`importLabel`, classified plain on purpose: a lead-source name from another system, not a field of a person; an email address or phone number typed into it is masked) and its digest, the review record's id, the old and the new source and the counts of AI care asked to pause, plans asked to stop and drafts withdrawn; the row's own actor and time say who and when. **Bounded work, no lost records.** A mapping reads its own label's people and records, never the team's, whatever its target: mapping to a marked source, confirming a suggestion that names one and turning the setting on start from the label's or the source's own people and join them to what there is to stop (`leads/sourceStop.ts`; until review round 5 these three asked a definer once for every active plan, live AI care and pending plan or alert draft of the whole team, E757); the CRM-wide list is one pass over the team's committed contacts import records. An import commit never turns a transient condition (a deadlock, a lock or statement timeout, a lost connection, a server out of resources) into a rejected record: the chunk fails as a whole and runs again. **Two consequences elsewhere.** A saved-search alert skips a person nothing automatic may go to BEFORE anything is queued or marked, and an alert already queued is released when dispatch refuses it for that reason, so nothing is recorded as alerted that was not sent (once the Owner reviews the source, the homes still on the market are alerted then). Their matches stay pending, so the sender must not ask the source check about them on every run: its due query holds no definer; it asks once per person, a page at a time, with no lock on them, and holds every active search of a refused person for an hour (`saved_searches.alerts_held_until`, migration `0104_b18_saved_search_alert_hold`), which the due query skips on a plain column, so held searches neither fill the batch nor cost later runs anything. The Owner's mapping change, a confirmed suggestion and the setting being turned off clear the holds of the people they may have released (`leads/sourceStop.ts` `releaseAlertHolds`), so those homes go on the sender's next run; a person released some other way (an import reverted) waits for the hold to run out, at most an hour. The Owner's request never waits for the sender to do this: it clears only the holds it can take at once (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), so a search the sender's open run has locked at that moment stays held until its hour runs out, and neither the Owner's request nor an inbound text or call on a tracking number of a lead source that request has locked waits for the sender's run (E758). A hold only ever withholds: the source check is asked again under the person's lock before anything is queued, and dispatch and the database guard still refuse (E756). An open-house invite asks the source check only for people whose detail the sender can see: a record seen only listed (a pond lead, an offered lead) or not at all gets the answer of an id that does not exist. **The screens (web).** "Review sources" is a step of every import, Follow Up Boss and file alike: from the snapshot until the contacts are imported it sits above the categories (the labels come from the snapshot, so it shows before any dry run and a preview still changes nothing), with every label exactly as imported (its spaces kept), its people, its state in words ("Mapped" only of a label a lead source holds; a suggestion is "In review · a match is suggested"), where it was first imported and who last changed its mapping, and the people with no label as their own row. It says plainly how much is still in review, that the import may run with labels left in review, and that those people get no automated contact until an Owner chooses a lead source while anyone on the team can still call, text or email them one to one; the contacts category repeats it next to Approve and Import, and once the contacts are imported the import's report carries the same review as its summary, counted by the people actually imported (the page shows the review once). The same list is the "Review sources" section of the Lead sources page, shown whatever the lead sources are, to which every import links. **Nothing is applied by a dropdown.** The Owner confirms a suggestion with its own button, or chooses a lead source and then presses "Map to …" / "Change to …" (the row first says what that will do), or removes a mapping after being told what going back in review stops and that nothing restarts on its own; the dropdown is never pre-set to a suggestion. Only the Owner gets controls (the list's `canChange`, or the Owner role on an import's page); an Admin reads the list and is told who maps, and an Owner with no lead source yet is told to add one.
