- **R-TEAMQ-2** **A new lead with no human attempt.** An INQUIRY is a `lead_inquiries` row whose outcome is not `duplicate_ignored` (whatever path it came by: a signed webhook, a lead email, a lead form, a partner feed, website chat, a text or call to a tracking number). An import and a person a member typed in write no inquiry and never appear. A HUMAN ATTEMPT is exactly the scoreboard's touch (migration 0053 `app.scoreboard_activity`): a call with `direction = 'outbound' AND initiator = 'human' AND NOT imported` (at `started_at`), or a message with `direction = 'outbound' AND initiator = 'human' AND NOT imported AND dispatched_at IS NOT NULL AND bulk_action_id IS NULL AND marketing_piece_id IS NULL` (a text, an email or a chat reply, at `dispatched_at`), on the person, by ANYONE. An automated message (initiator `assistant` or `system`), a bulk send, an open-house invite and imported history are never attempts; an automated reply is said apart in the row and never clears it. The group lists every inquiry of the last 14 days (core `TEAM_QUEUE_NEW_LEAD_WINDOW_MS`) whose person has had no human attempt at or after the inquiry's `received_at`, whoever holds the lead: owned, on offer, in a pond or unassigned. Anything but owned reads "No owner yet" with where the lead is. One person is one row, counted from their first unanswered arrival. The wait is counted from ARRIVAL (`received_at`), never from when an agent accepted, in wall-clock time and in STAFFED time: the seconds inside the tenant's routing hours (`tenant_settings.routing_hours_start` to `routing_hours_end` in `default_timezone`, every day); an arrival outside those hours counts staffed time from the next opening and is marked. A closed or deleted person is never listed. A person from a "No automated contact" source (R-LSRC-8) is listed like any other: the queue is about human work. [core + api + web]
- **R-TEAMQ-3** **An unanswered reply** is decided by the agent feed's own predicate (core `answersClient`): the person's inbound message or missed call of the last 14 days, not imported, not an auto-reply, not an opt-out or HELP keyword, with no later human message that passed the gate and no later call a person picked up. An assistant or system message never answers. Read for every person the viewer's scope holds, whoever has the lead; one row per person, aged from the first waiting message. [core + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-4** **A due promise and an overdue follow-up** use the feed's deadline (core `taskDeadline`: the end of the local day for an all-day task) and list only PAST-DUE open tasks about a person: a promise in the first group, any other kind (follow-up, callback, to-do, document) in the second. A task with no person, a task not yet due, and a task an import created (R-IMPORT-6) are never listed. The row is on the member the task is assigned to. Tasks that came due more than 90 days ago are not read. [core + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-5** **An appointment needing an outcome** is listed as soon as it has ended (core `awaitsOutcome`: scheduled or held, no outcome logged, not imported), not two hours later as the `missing_outcome` exception (R-ACC-2). The row is on the member whose appointment it is. Appointments that ended more than 30 days ago are not read. [core + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-6** **Who reads the queue and the report.** `team_queue.view`: Owner and Admin, and a Team Lead for their teams; an Agent, an ISA and a TC get 404 (R-PERM-5); never Support or the system. A Team Lead's rows are the leads owned by, or on offer to, themself or a member of a team they lead, and leads in a pond (their own RLS already shows them every pond lead); the task and appointment groups hold items on those same members. An unassigned lead is the Owner's and Admin's to see. Every read runs as the caller under row-level security, so no row names a person the caller may not see, and nothing crosses tenants. No input carries a tenant id (R-TEN-3). [core + api + web]
- **R-TEAMQ-7** **The lead response report measures from arrival** (`GET /team/lead-response?range=today|7d|30d|90d`; read-only; the scope of R-TEAMQ-6). Per inquiry (R-TEAMQ-2) received in the period: its arrival; the FIRST human attempt (R-TEAMQ-2) on the person at or after that arrival, by anyone; who made it and their role to the lead at that moment: `owner` when the ledger's latest event at or before the attempt names them as the owner, `collaborator` when `contact_collaborators` held them then, else `other`; the time from arrival in staffed and in wall-clock seconds; and when an automated reply went out, shown apart and never counted. An off-hours arrival is counted from opening and marked. Two inquiries from one person are two arrivals, each with its own first attempt after it. Inquiries of people since closed are left out and counted apart; people an import added in the period are counted apart; neither is in the measure. A period holding more than 5,000 inquiries says the numbers cover the newest only. [core + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-8** **The roll-up by lead source carries every denominator** (R-ACC-4): inquiries; with a human attempt; within 5 minutes and within 15 minutes, meaning the staffed time from arrival to the first human attempt is at most 300 or 900 seconds; still waiting; with an automatic reply (apart). The denominator of each is every inquiry of the source in the period, so an inquiry still waiting counts against it. The median and the 90th percentile (nearest rank) of staffed seconds are over attempted inquiries only, always with that sample size, and NEVER on a small sample: no median below 5 attempts, no 90th percentile below 10 (core `RESPONSE_MEDIAN_MIN_SAMPLE`, `RESPONSE_P90_MIN_SAMPLE`). The inquiries not within 5 minutes are listed under their source, still waiting first, at most 20 with a count of the rest. [core + api + web]
- **R-TEAMQ-9** **The report says what it is.** On the scoreboard page it is headed "Lead response, from arrival" and says in one line how it differs from the board's speed to lead, which starts when an agent accepts (R-ACC-4, `v_speed_to_lead`). Only Owner, Admin and Team Lead are shown it, and no other role's page asks for it. [web]
- **R-TEAMQ-10** **Source coverage** (b19 step 2; migration `0107_b19_source_coverage`; owner decisions of 2026-11-19). Each lead source can carry a named OWNER and a named BACKUP (members of the same team), a HIGH INTENT mark and a COVERED switch. The switch can be on only while an owner and a backup are both named (the API refuses with `coverage_needs_owner_and_backup`; the database holds the same check). A source is COVERED NOW only while the switch is on AND both people are active members (core `sourceCoverage`). The state is always said in words: "Covered: Jordan P. owns this source and Sam T. is the backup." or "Not covered: no backup is named." `GET` and `PUT /lead-sources/:id/coverage` and the `coverage` of every source in `GET /lead-sources/overview` return the two people (id, name, membership status), the mark, the stored switch, whether it is covered now, the sentence and the warnings (R-TEAMQ-11). Owner and Admin read and change it (`settings.routing`, the permission of the other source settings; every other role gets 404, R-PERM-5); every change writes one audit row with from and to (ids and switches only), and saying the same again writes nothing. A member being NEWLY named must be an active member who can take leads (never a TC, R-PERM-15); a member or a source of another team is 404. High intent is a mark for people and reports: it changes no routing. Naming an owner or a backup moves no lead and changes nobody's leads. [core + db + api + web]
- **R-TEAMQ-11** **Coverage warnings; a named member who is suspended or deactivated.** The read computes warnings, each a code and a sentence a person can act on: `NO_OWNER`; `NO_BACKUP`; `OWNER_UNAVAILABLE` and `BACKUP_UNAVAILABLE` (the named person is suspended, deactivating, removed or not yet joined); `SAME_PERSON` (owner and backup are one person: nobody else is alerted when they do not respond); `NO_ROUTING_RULE` (no enabled routing rule names this source, or the rule that names it sits behind a rule with no condition and is never reached: its leads follow the first rule that names no source, else go straight to the default pond; core `sourceRouting`, read from the router's own rule facts); `SHARED_FALLBACK_POND` (the pond its leads fall back to is also the fallback pond of another source that is on). None of them changes a lead. Suspending or deactivating a member who is a source's owner or backup NEVER fails because of it and writes nothing to the source: the row keeps naming them, the read flags it and says "Not covered: the backup, Sam T., is suspended.", and nobody is put in their place; reactivating them covers the source again; the Owner or Admin can still save the other fields with the unavailable person left as named, but cannot newly name them. [core + db + api + web]
- **R-TEAMQ-12** **A pond arrival alerts the team leads** (owner decision 4). When an ownership decision leaves a lead in a pond it was not in before, through routing (the rule sends it there, no rule matched, every offer expired, nobody was available), a release by its owner, or the first-touch auto-release, every active Team Lead of the tenant gets ONE `pond_new` notification at high priority by the existing notification path (in-app and push; high priority sends no text); a tenant with no Team Lead alerts its Owners and Admins instead. Ponds belong to no team and a Team Lead sees every pond, so "the pond's team leads" is the tenant's team leads. It is decided in core with the move (`decide.ts` `pondArrival`), so the notification is an effect of the decision and commits in the same transaction as the ledger event, keyed `pond_new:{contact}:{seq of the arriving event}`: a replayed command, a redelivered timer and a replayed intake alert nobody twice, and a later arrival is its own alert. Fewest alerts: the person who made the move is not told about their own move, and a person the same decision already alerts (the critical `NO_ELIGIBLE_AGENT` notice; the auto-release notice to the owner's Team Lead) gets no second notification for the same arrival. NOT an arrival, so no alert: an import (R-OWN-13), a merge, a re-inquiry that stays in the pond, the inactivity release of an old lead, and a member's deactivation (an Owner's or Admin's own bulk move). The title is PII-free and says why it arrived. Members only: nothing is sent to the client. [core + db]
- **R-TEAMQ-13** **A covered source's lead is not auto-released** (owner decision 3, for the pilot; R-TEAMQ-20 adds that the lead's own owner must be an active member and that the waiver is re-evaluated). When the `ownership.auto_release` timer fires (R-OWN-13) for a lead whose source is covered now (R-TEAMQ-10), nothing is released: the lead stays with its owner, and the stored release deadline is cleared by one ledgered `sla_waived` event (reason `AUTO_RELEASE_OFF_COVERED_SOURCE`, payload `releaseDueAt: null`), so the sweeper has nothing left to ask and no screen promises a return to the pond. Nobody is notified by it. PRECEDENCE: (1) a first touch already recorded, or a stale period or deadline: nothing to release; (2) the source is covered now: no release, whatever `first_touch_release_seconds` says; (3) otherwise the tenant setting decides exactly as before. The tenant setting itself is unchanged. The first-touch breach is a separate timer on the same period and still fires and alerts the owner and their Team Lead. Coverage is read WHEN THE TIMER FIRES, not when the period started (an exception to "nothing is re-evaluated" of R-OWN-15 that only ever lets the owner keep the lead): the lead's source is the source of the person's latest inquiry that is not an ignored duplicate, else the source they first came from. A source switched off since, or whose owner or backup is no longer active, is not covered, and its lead is released as before. [core + db]
- **R-TEAMQ-14** **The minute-three backup alert: when it is queued and when it runs** (owner decision 1: it ALERTS ONLY). At intake, every inbound inquiry that is an arrival (`new_contact`, `attached`, `reinquiry`; never `duplicate_ignored`; never an import, which writes no inquiry) of a source whose coverage switch is on queues ONE job `leads.backup_alert { inquiryId }` in the intake transaction, due after three minutes of STAFFED time (the tenant's routing hours, R-TEAMQ-2) counted from the later of the arrival the source reported and the moment the lead reached the team; an off-hours arrival counts from opening. A second inquiry of the same person is its own arrival with its own job. When the job runs it decides ONCE for that inquiry and records why (R-TEAMQ-17): `lead_closed` (the person was closed, deleted or merged away); `source_not_covered` (the switch was turned off in between: nothing happens); `human_attempt` (a person has called, texted, emailed or replied in chat since the arrival: exactly the human attempt of R-TEAMQ-2, by anyone; an automated reply NEVER counts and never cancels the alert, R-AST-15); `answered_call` (a person answered the lead's own call since the arrival); else it alerts (R-TEAMQ-15) or records `no_recipient`. Woken before it is due, it writes nothing and sleeps until then. It issues no ownership command: the owner, the offer, its deadline and the pond are exactly what they were. It sends, drafts and queues nothing for the client, also for a "No automated contact" source, whose staff are still alerted. [core + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-15** **Who is alerted, and what the alert says** (owner decision 2; the recipient rule is now R-TEAMQ-21: never the person holding the lead, and the Owners and Admins when there is no team lead). The source's named backup, when an active member. When the backup is absent (not an active member: suspended, deactivating, removed, not yet joined), the Team Lead of whoever has the lead (its owner, else the member it is on offer to; the first by id when there are several), and the source reads as flagged (R-TEAMQ-11); when there is no such Team Lead (a lead in a pond or unassigned, or a holder in no led team) nobody is alerted and the row says `no_recipient`. The alert is a member's notification by the existing critical path: kind `first_touch_due`, priority `critical`: in-app, push to their devices, and a text to their verified alert phone. Its TITLE (120 characters at most; what the push and the text carry) holds the whole alert: "New lead waiting 3 min: Dana R. from Website form. With Jordan P. (on offer). You are the backup." (the client as first name and last initial, or not at all under the team's `notification_detail` setting; the lead source; the wall-clock wait; who has it: with the owner, on offer to someone, in which pond, or not assigned; a long source name is cut with an ellipsis). The body adds "Nobody has called, texted or emailed." and, for an off-hours arrival, the staffed time apart. The fallback's title ends "The source's backup is unavailable." Never a phone number, an address or message text. [core + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-16** **The backup's access is one lead, time-boxed.** A backup is usually neither the lead's owner nor a collaborator, so without more they could not open the lead the alert names. With the alert, the named backup gets a `backup` collaborator row on that one lead (migration 0108 widens the role check) that expires 24 hours later, audited as the system's (`collaborator.added`, reason `BACKUP_ALERT`). It is the existing collaborator mechanism, unchanged: that member sees that person's card and detail and may call or text them (the send-time gate applies as always), sees no other lead because of it, and sees nothing once it has expired; no other member's sight changes. It also works on a lead still ON OFFER, in a pond or unassigned (a collaborator row needs no owner); there a call or a text by the backup is made and stored under the backup's own id, and NO first touch is written to the ownership ledger, because a first touch belongs to an ownership period and nobody owns the lead yet (R-OWN-14). On an owned lead the backup's attempt IS the first touch, recorded under the backup's id with the owner unchanged. The OWNER's own human attempt ends the grant at once, in the owner's own transaction (core `endBackupAccess`; the row stays as the record); the backup's own attempt does not end it. A member who already collaborates on the lead in another role keeps that access exactly as it was. A Team Lead alerted as the fallback gets no grant: they already reach their team's leads. [core + db + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-17** **One decision per inquiry, recorded.** `lead_backup_alerts` (migration 0108) holds ONE row per inquiry (`lead_backup_alerts_inquiry_once`): the outcome, who was alerted and whether as the backup or as the fallback Team Lead, when the inquiry arrived, when the alert was due and when it was decided, who had the lead then, and until when the backup's access runs. Running the job twice, a redelivery and a replayed intake leave one row, one notification and one job. The row is written by the system actor only and never changed or deleted by the application; it holds ids, codes and instants only. It is read by the tenant-wide, by the alerted member, and by whoever can read its inquiry; nothing crosses tenants, and another team's job cannot decide this team's inquiry. Support reads with a grant and never writes. [db + api]
- **R-TEAMQ-18** **The queue and the report show the backup alert.** A new-lead row of the team work queue (R-TEAMQ-2) whose minute-three alert was raised carries its own line: "Backup alerted at 2:06 PM: Sam T." (the time in the team's zone, as the why line's; the fallback and "Nobody could be alerted …" in their own words), and the group says how many of ALL its waiting rows had their backup alerted ("Backup alerted for 1 of 4."). In the lead response report (R-TEAMQ-7), an attempt made by the person this inquiry's alert went to, at or after the alert, has the role `backup`, distinct from `owner` (the owner at that moment is always `owner`), `collaborator` and `other`, under their own id; an inquiry says who was alerted and how long after it arrived; and every roll-up (R-TEAMQ-8) counts "backup alerted" over its inquiries and "first attempt by the backup" over those alerted, the second shown only when there is something to divide by (R-ACC-4). Both reads see an alert exactly when the caller can see its inquiry. [core + api + web]
- **R-TEAMQ-19** **One arrival instant** (b19 review fix M3). An inquiry carries two times: the time its source claims (`lead_inquiries.received_at`; intake keeps a claim up to 5 minutes ahead of the team's clock and up to 7 days behind it) and the moment it reached the team (`lead_inquiries.created_at`: the first moment anybody on the team could see it). ARRIVAL is ONE instant, defined once (core `priority/arrival.ts` `arrivalInstant`; its SQL twin `ARRIVAL` in `accountability/repos/teamQueue.ts`): the later of the two, where a claim can never be later than the reach. A claim before the reach (delivered late, or back-dated) arrives at the reach: a text sent before the lead reached the team is not a response to it, and the team's three minutes start when it has it. A claim after the reach (the source's clock runs ahead) is impossible, because a lead cannot arrive after the team already has it, so it reads as the reach: a text one second after the lead reached the team is a response to it. So the arrival equals the reach, and the claim is kept on the row as what the source said. Everything counted "from arrival" reads this one definition: when the minute-three alert is due (R-TEAMQ-14), "a human attempt since the arrival" in the alert job, the queue and the report, the queue's wait (R-TEAMQ-2), the report's response times and the period an inquiry falls in (R-TEAMQ-7, R-TEAMQ-8), and `lead_backup_alerts.arrived_at`. The report's field is `arrivedAt`. Before this fix the due time used max(claim, reach) and every attempt check used the claim alone, so a fast source clock hid the owner's text and alerted the backup, and a back-dated second inquiry was credited with an earlier text.
- **R-TEAMQ-20** **The auto-release waiver needs the lead's own owner, and is re-evaluated** (b19 review fix M1; migration `0109_b19_team_queue_review_fixes`). The release of R-TEAMQ-13 is waived only while the lead's source is covered now (R-TEAMQ-24) AND the lead's own owner is an active member (core `autoReleaseWaivedNow`). A lead held by a suspended or deactivating member is released as on an uncovered source, at its stored release time, and the pond arrival tells the team leads, or the Owners and Admins of a team with no Team Lead (R-TEAMQ-12). The waiver is not final: when its reason goes (a source's coverage is switched off; a member stops being active, as a lead's owner or as a source's named owner or backup), the change queues one `leads.release_recheck` job in its own transaction, which asks core for every owned, untouched lead whose release was waived in its current ownership period (`release_recheck`, a system-only command). A waiver that still holds is left alone. One that does not gets a ledgered `sla_restored` event that stores a NEW release deadline: the tenant's `first_touch_release_seconds` of staffed time counted from that moment (the settings in force then; none when the tenant turned the auto-release off), with the period's ordinary `ownership.auto_release` timer. That timer decides again when it fires: covered again by then, the release is waived again; else the lead goes to the default pond. The stored deadline is `contacts.release_due_at`, which `app.due_work` reads, so the sweeper re-issues a lost timer as for any deadline. The re-check moves no lead and notifies nobody.
- **R-TEAMQ-21** **A waiting covered lead always tells someone other than the person holding it** (b19 review fix M2; replaces the recipient rule of R-TEAMQ-15). Core `backupAlertRecipients`: (1) the source's named backup, when an active member and NOT the person holding the lead (its owner or offeree); (2) else the first active team lead of whoever holds the lead, never the holder themself, recorded as `team_lead` with `fallback_reason` `backup_unavailable` or `backup_is_holder`; (3) else the team's active Owners and Admins except the holder, each with their own notification, recorded as `admins` with `alerted_count` (a lead in a pond or unassigned, or a holder on no team, has no team lead to tell; this replaces the old "nobody could be alerted"); (4) `no_recipient` only when the team has nobody else at all. When the backup is the holder the alert reads "New lead waiting 3 min: Dana R. from Website form. With Jordan P., who is also the backup for this source." and its body says why this person hears it. Only the named backup gets the time-boxed access of R-TEAMQ-16. The coverage read warns `BACKUP_IN_ROTATION` when the named backup is in the rotation of the rule the source's leads follow: "The backup also receives these leads. When a lead is with them, their team lead is alerted instead."
- **R-TEAMQ-22** **A person answering the lead's own call is its own state** (b19 review fix M4). An inbound call a person answered (`calls.direction = 'inbound'`, `answered_at` set, `handled_by = 'human'`, not imported) is never a human attempt: nobody reached out. It counts for an inquiry when it started at or after the arrival, or when it IS the inquiry: the call that creates a call-in inquiry on a tracked number starts a moment before the inquiry reaches the team, so it is matched by the inquiry's own reference (`lead_inquiries.external_id = 'tracking:call:' || calls.provider_call_ref`). One predicate serves the alert job (outcome `answered_call`), the queue and the report. The queue keeps the row (R-TEAMQ-2: no attempt yet) and says the truth: "A person answered their call at 2:04 PM. Nobody has called back, texted or emailed since."; the group counts those rows; the group's label is "New leads nobody has called, texted or emailed yet" (was "New leads nobody has tried yet"). The report gives every inquiry a state, `attempted`, else `answered_call`, else `waiting`, and the roll-up counts answered calls apart (attempted + answered calls + waiting = inquiries). An answered call is never within 5 or 15 minutes; a later call back is the attempt and both facts are shown.
- **R-TEAMQ-23** **A first touch made by the backup is the backup's, never the owner's own response** (b19 review fix M5; migration 0109). The ledger is unchanged: `first_touch_recorded` on the owner's period, with the backup as its actor (R-TEAMQ-16). What reads it says whose it was. "By the backup" is the report's rule (R-TEAMQ-18): made by someone other than the period's owner who had been alerted as the `backup` of an inquiry of that person at or before the touch. `v_speed_to_lead` gains `first_touch_user_id` and `first_touch_by_backup`, and for such a period `response_seconds` is NULL and `within_sla` false (`first_touch_at` still says when the lead was first touched). The scoreboard marks its speed rows from `app.scoreboard_backup_first_touches` (the scoreboard's own member rule; timings only) and core `speedToLeadSummary` counts those periods as `byBackup`: outside `total`, `withinSla`, `responded` and the median. The owner's speed to lead does not improve when the backup made the call, and the period is not held against them either (the ledger records one first touch per period, so their own later call cannot be measured). The page shows "First touch by the backup: x of y".
- **R-TEAMQ-24** **One definition of "covered now"** (b19 review fix L3). Core `coverageNow({ setting, ownerStatus, backupStatus })` is the one table: `covered` (the switch on and the named owner and backup both active); `alert` (`off` while the switch is off; while it is on the alert ALWAYS tells someone: `backup` when the named backup is active, else `fallback`, R-TEAMQ-21, also when the source's own owner is unavailable); `autoRelease` (`waived` only while covered). The coverage card, the auto-release (R-TEAMQ-20) and the alert job read it. The card carries `effects`, two sentences of what happens to this source's leads now, with the team's own release time (`first_touch_release_seconds`, never a hard-coded 30 minutes; said too when the team turned the automatic return off), and the page lists them under "What happens now".
- **R-TEAMQ-25** **An alert that went to a team lead is the team lead's** (b19 review fix L4). In the report, the role of an attempter this inquiry's alert went to is the role they were alerted in: `backup` only for the source's named backup, `team_lead` for a team lead alerted in the backup's place, `admin` for an Owner or Admin alerted when there was no team lead. Roll-ups count `backupAlerted` and `byBackup` for the backup only, and apart `teamLeadAlerted`, `byTeamLead` and `adminsAlerted`. A queue row's alert says who it went to, and the group counts `backupAlerted` and `othersAlerted` apart ("Backup alerted for 1 of 2. A team lead or the Owners and Admins were alerted instead for 1 of 2.").
- **R-TEAMQ-26** **A merge carries the waiting inquiry and the backup's live grant** (b19 review fix L5). A merge step (`leads/mergeStep.ts`; in the merge's move transaction, as the same tenant-wide actor, like the messages and calls of R-OWN-24) re-points the inquiries of every record the merge brings in to the survivor (`lead_inquiries.contact_id` only: the inquiry keeps its id, source, times and outcome, so its job, its `lead_backup_alerts` row and its queue and report rows follow), and carries a LIVE `backup` grant to the survivor with the same expiry, ending it on the old record. Another role the member holds on the survivor is left as it is; an expired, ended or removed grant is not revived. Records merged before this fix are picked up by the next merge of their person.
- **R-TEAMQ-27** **A send the provider reported as failed** (b19 review fix L6). A human text, email or chat reply whose status is `failed`, `undelivered` or `bounced` is still the scoreboard's touch (0053), and the report still shows it as the first attempt, marked `failed` ("Tried, but the text failed: it did not reach them."), with a count of failed attempts. It does NOT take the lead out of the queue (the row says "Jordan P. tried at 2:05 PM, but the text failed. Nobody else has called, texted or emailed.") and does NOT stop the minute-three alert (its body says "A text was tried, but it failed. Nobody else has called, texted or emailed."). The job decides once per inquiry: a failure the carrier reports only after minute three raises no late alert; the queue, read live, lists the lead again.
- **R-TEAMQ-28** **Smaller rules of the review** (b19 review fix L7; migration 0109). (a) A lost alert job is re-issued: the periodic `leads.backup_alert_sweep` (every 5 minutes, one per team) gives every inquiry of a covered source that reached the team in the last two days, is a minute past due and has no decision row its job again, under the inquiry's own dedupe key (never a second live job; it alerts nobody itself). (b) A live `backup` grant ends when the lead leaves its owner (reassigned, released, auto-released, closed, deleted, deactivation), not on a merge (R-TEAMQ-26). (c) A `backup` collaborator row always has an expiry (`contact_collaborators_backup_time_boxed`); a person's manual share of that member is an explicit change to the role the sharer chose (`isa`, `tc`, `co_agent`, never `backup`) with its own `collaborator.added` audit entry by that person. (d) The report reads the inquiries of people since deleted, never measures them, and counts them (`deletedExcluded`, "2 inquiries from people since deleted are left out."). Open questions, not built: batching the pond-arrival alert (R-TEAMQ-12 sends one high-priority notification per arrival to each team lead: a burst of 50 routed pond arrivals with 3 team leads is 150 notifications, in the app and as push, no text); whether a later alert for another inquiry of the same person should extend the backup's 24 hours (it does today); naming the replaced backup grant in the manual share's audit entry (`apps/api/src/people`, another lane's file).
- **R-TEAMQ-29** **The two queue reads are bounded** (b19 review step 7; migration 0109). The waiting-inquiries read ranges on the arrival per lead source through `lead_inquiries_source_arrival_idx (tenant_id, lead_source_id, created_at DESC)`, with every filter that decides "waiting" inside the per-source subquery and its own limit (cap + 1, oldest first). The waiting-inbound read of a caller who is not tenant-wide starts from their scope, each branch on its own index (`contacts_owner_idx` and `contacts_offeree_idx` for their members, the activity index for pond leads), instead of walking the tenant's whole recently active book; the scope predicate stays on the outer row. `accountability/teamQueue.perf.test.ts` holds the plans.

### Stage flow (batch 19; migration `0105_b19_stage_undo`)

The eight stage keys and their model are unchanged (R-PAR-13 to R-PAR-16). These rules say what a stage means, what a logged outcome suggests, what a stage change will do before it is saved and did after, and that an undo starts nothing. Security notes: ARCHITECTURE §21.72.

- **R-STG-1** **Stages describe what is true, and a label is display only.** Each of the eight keys has a default label, a one-line definition and a one-line "does not mean" (core `STAGE_GUIDE`): New lead, Trying to reach ("We tried to reach them but have not had a two-way conversation yet."), Connected ("We have talked, but we are not actively working together yet."), Long-term follow-up, Active client ("We are working together now."), Under contract, Closed sale, Not moving forward. Each default label fits the 24 characters a team label may have. A team's own label (R-PAR-13) still wins for the label; the definition and the "does not mean" are the guide's. `GET /stage-labels`, `PUT /stage-labels` and the contact detail carry them (`stages[].definition`, `stages[].notThis`, `details.stage.definition`, `details.stage.notThis`, `details.stageGuide`). A stage says how far the relationship has come: buyer or seller, urgency and permission to text are separate facts. No trigger, enrollment, rule or permission reads a label: renaming the stages, even to each other's names, starts and stops nothing, and a label is refused wherever a key is expected. [core + api]
- **R-STG-2** **A logged outcome suggests one stage to confirm; it never changes the stage.** Core `suggestStageFromOutcome({ current, signal })` is the whole table: a voicemail, no answer, an "other" call, or a text or email sent with no reply suggests `attempting` only from `new`; a connected call, a callback request or the person's own reply suggests `connected` only from `new` or `attempting`; a wrong number, a bad number and a do-not-call request suggest nothing; the appointment result `agreement_signed` suggests `active` from `new`, `attempting`, `connected` or `nurture`. Nothing else suggests `active`; `nurture`, `under_contract`, `closed` and `lost` are never the suggested stage; a suggestion is never an earlier stage than the current one. A two-way conversation (a connected call, the person's own reply) also carries `alsoOffer: ['nurture']`, so the screen can offer "Long-term follow-up" beside "Connected". `POST /contacts/:id/activities` and `POST /calls/:id/outcome` answer `suggestedStage: { stage, why, locked, alsoOffer: [{ stage, locked }] } | null`. `locked` is the stage rule's own answer for the caller (core `stageChangeAllowed`, R-PAR-14), never a second rule. Nothing is suggested to someone who does not see the person, to a caller without `contact.edit`, on a merged-away record, or for an outcome handed to the new owner. An incoming call counts only when it connected, and a call logged with no outcome suggests nothing. Neither route writes a stage. [core + api]
- **R-STG-3** **The preview of a stage change is the change's own decision, and writes nothing.** `GET /contacts/:id/stage/preview?to=<stage>` has the stage route's permission and visibility (`contact.edit` on a live person whose detail the caller sees) and its stage rule. The facts of a stage entry are read by one function (`stageAutomation/reconcile.ts` `readEntryFacts`) and decided by one (`decideEntry`: those facts, then the pure `decideStageEntry`); the reconcile and the preview both call `decideEntry`. The preview describes an entry a person would make now and reads everything else (who sees the person, the live enrollment, AI care, the source restrictions, opt-outs and reachability, approval) exactly as the reconcile does, in a READ ONLY transaction as the system actor: no decision row, enrollment, job, message or audit row. It answers `allowed`, `lockedReason`, `sameStage`, `starts { plan, firstStep { channel, dueAt } }`, `wontStart { plan, reason, sentence, alreadyIn }` (for `cannot_be_reached` the sentence names the cause read with those same facts: an opt-out, do not contact, a wrong number, or no usable phone or email; E787), `keepsRunning { enrollmentId, plan, state, canStop }`, `stops: []` (a stage change never stops a plan), `aiCare: 'unchanged'` and `tasks: 'unchanged'`. The first step is the step runner's own planning walk. A plan the viewer's own RLS does not show is named "the owner's plan". Consent, STOP, do-not-contact, the source restrictions and plan approval are decided exactly as for the change: the preview can only repeat a refusal, never lift one. [api]
- **R-STG-4** **A contact's playbook status says what is running, what is next, and what the last stage change did.** `GET /contacts/:id/playbook-status` (`contact.view_detail`) answers the person's live enrollment, else the most recent ended one (`plan { enrollmentId, name, state, startedBy, startedAt, next, blocked, deliveryUnconfirmed, canPause, canResume, canStop }`), and the person's most recent stage event with its decision (`lastStageChange { eventId, to, at, pending, decision { outcome, plan, reason, sentence } }`). `next` is the next real step by the step runner's own planning walk (`assistant/stepWalk.ts` `walkSteps`, which the runner acts on), never `next_step_due_at`, and null when no step is next. `blocked` is why a paused or stopped plan is not moving; a paused plan's sentence invites "Resume" only when `canResume` is true (E786), and a `cannot_be_reached` decision's sentence names its stored cause (E787). `canPause`, `canResume` and `canStop` are the pause, resume and stop routes' own authorization (`comms.send` on the person as a marketing send) and their own state checks (`pauseCheck`, `resumeCheck`, `stopCheck`, which those commands act on), read in the viewer's transaction: a button is offered exactly when the viewer's request to that route would act. `pending` is the backup scan's own query for that event: an undecided change is never reported as started. `deliveryUnconfirmed` is set when the plan's most recent message was handed to the provider and no answer came (R-GATE-8): it is never called sent, delivered, not delivered or retried. Read-only. [api]
- **R-STG-5** **An undo never starts a plan, and never stops one; a repeated stage change is one change.** `POST /contacts/:id/stage` answers the stage event it wrote (`eventId`). `undoOf` marks the change as the undo of the person's MOST RECENT stage event, back to the stage that event came from; anything else (another person's or team's event, an older or unknown one, another stage) is 409 `STAGE_UNDO_INVALID` and nothing changes. An undo is only an undo (E779): the named event must be the caller's own change, must not itself be an undo, and must be at most 15 minutes old; otherwise the same 409, and the same move without `undoOf` is an ordinary stage change. The mark is written on the stage event in the transaction that changes the stage (`contact_stage_events.undo_of_event_id`, written only by the history definer, which keeps it only for a user's true reversal of the contact's latest event under those same three conditions; the API rolls the change back if the event it wrote does not carry the mark), so the request path and the worker's backup scan both decide it the same way: skipped, reason `undo`. An undo leaves a running plan running and a paused plan paused: the answer names it (`stillRunning { enrollmentId, plan, state, canStop }`; E782), and stopping it is the stop route. The stage answer also carries `automationSentence`, the decision in the words the status uses (E784). The same stage again (a double tap, a refresh, a retry, the other of two taps at once) writes nothing and answers the event that put the person there and its stored decision; an older undecided event is never decided by a repeat. [DB, api]
- **R-STG-6** **A task saved twice is one task.** `POST /tasks` takes an optional client-generated `id` (the `noteId` replay pattern of R-PAR-30's log route). The same id again by the same person for the same contact answers the stored task (200, `replayed: true`) and writes nothing, whatever the retried body says. An id that names a task the caller did not make, or their own task for another contact, is 422 `COMMAND_ID_REUSED` whether or not they can see that task: the id never reads and never overwrites it. Another tenant's task with the same id is a different row. [api]
- **R-STG-7** **A contact's stage never rewrites a deal, and a deal's stage never moves the person.** One person can have several deals in different deal stages; moving the person through every stage key ("closed", "lost" and "under_contract" included) changes no deal row, deal history, deal date or deal contact. [api]

### Warm follow-up in Today (batch 19; migration `0110_b19_follow_up_history_idx`, indexes only)

Follow-up by people, not more automation. Today says, for each person an agent is due to follow up with, why they are due, what was last discussed and the next promise; from Today or from any screen the agent logs a call or conversation and sets the next step in one pass, a call made from their own phone included. Nothing here sends a message, creates one, changes how an attempt is scored or moves the first-contact clock; every send still goes through `comms/dispatch.ts`.

- **R-TODAY-1** **A follow-up card says why the person is due, what was last discussed and the next promise.** `GET /feed` adds, on a card about following up with a person (first contact due, reply waiting, missed call, handback, promise due, task due, re-inquiry, inactivity warning, and the rows of R-TODAY-6), `why` (the card's own reason sentences, unchanged), `stage` (key and the team's label), `lastTouch`, `lastAutomatic`, `importedHistoryAt` and `nextPromise`; every one is null on any other card. `lastTouch` is the most recent HUMAN contact the viewer may see: `{ kind: call|text|email|chat|note|appointment, at, byName, byMe, direction, outcome, snippet, reported }`. The snippet is at most 140 characters of the agent's own note or of the message. The selection is pure (core `priority/followUp.ts` `selectLastContact`); a person with none says "No conversation yet." (never under a Due line that already says it: R-TODAY-14). A lead-offer card and a pond card carry no such lines, and neither does any card about a person the viewer cannot open (R-TODAY-16). Since the fix pass the card also carries `lastConversation` (R-TODAY-12) and `quiet` (R-TODAY-14). [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-2** **An automated message is never the last thing discussed.** A message the assistant or a plan sent, and any bulk or marketing send, is never `lastTouch`, whatever its date or the order of the rows. The most recent one after the last human contact is `lastAutomatic` (`{ kind, at, bulk }`), shown as its own line ("Automatic text sent Tue"); its text is never selected, sent or shown. A queued, scheduled, blocked, failed or unconfirmed message, an auto-reply, a missed or assistant-answered call, the assistant's call summary and an appointment that was not held are not a contact at all. Imported history is neither: only the date of its latest entry is said, and only when there is no last touch. [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-3** **The next promise is the viewer's earliest open task for the person, as written, and overdue is decided in the team's time zone.** `nextPromise` is `{ taskId, text, kind, dueAt, dueAllDay, dueZone, deadlineAt, overdue }` or null (`deadlineAt`: when it stops being on time, read by R-TODAY-11; `dueZone`: R-TODAY-19). An all-day task is on time until the end of its day in the task's own zone, else the team's (`tenant_settings.default_timezone`); an undated task comes last and is never overdue. [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-4** **A logged outside call is the agent's own report and is labelled so wherever it is shown.** It is a note (`source = logged_activity`, R-PAR-30), never a `calls` row, never a touch, and it moves no clock the system keeps (R-PAR-32). As a last touch it carries `reported: true` and no duration; Today's card, the person's notes and the timeline all word it from one function ("Logged by you: called from your phone, left a voicemail", with "not verified" on the card, in the notes and in the timeline). It is never worded as a call the system placed. How it counts on a scoreboard is not decided here. [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-5** **Nothing reaches a viewer past row-level security, and the reads are bounded.** The lines are read in the viewer's own transaction (messages, calls, notes and appointments each show a row only with the person's detail) and only for people whose detail the viewer may open; no definer reads on anyone's behalf. A stored redaction is quoted as stored, and a sign-in token stored before the redaction existed is hidden before the text is cut. The teammate's id is not sent (`byMe` is). One page of cards costs 3 statements whatever its size (one more only when a quoted note mentions a teammate); the whole of R-TODAY-6 with it, 7 when one page of candidates is enough and 6 more for each further page (R-TODAY-13). Every history read is an index read by person (R-TODAY-17). [api]
- **R-TODAY-6** **"Follow up today" is the agent's daily plan for warm people.** `GET /feed` adds `followUpToday: { people, total, totalIsMinimum, checked, complete }` (the last two: R-TODAY-13): people the viewer OWNS who are due by a task of the viewer's today or earlier (the team's day), or who are in "Connected" or "Long-term follow-up" with no human contact for longer than the stage's threshold (core `WARM_FOLLOW_UP_DAYS`: 7 and 30 days) and no dated next step. A logged outside call and imported history count as contact for this purpose only; a manual note never does (R-TODAY-12). Most overdue first, at most 20, with the total. A person with several due tasks is one row, which leads with a due promise (R-TODAY-15). Never someone who opted out of every channel they have, someone whose record is closed or merged away, or another agent's person. A source marked "No automated contact" removes nobody: it restricts automated contact, and this is the agent's own one-to-one follow-up. It is read with the feed and never stored; reading it writes nothing and creates no message. A person is drawn once: their task cards leave "Next up" for the row, except a promise the row does not show (R-TODAY-15). [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-7** **One pass from a follow-up card.** On Today a follow-up card's Call, Text and Log open the person page's own sheets (the call sheet, the text sheet, the Log sheet), each going on to the same stage step (R-STG-2, R-STG-3), without leaving Today. In a one-pass sheet the follow-up the outcome suggests is preselected (core `suggestedFollowUpDays`: tomorrow after a voicemail, no answer or a call-back request; 3 days after a text or email with no reply; a week after a conversation; none otherwise), said in words, named under the one button in the sheet's pinned footer (R-TODAY-11), and removable with None; nothing is created before the one button is tapped. Whether the task the card is about is marked done by the same tap is decided by the one rule of R-TODAY-11, and said before the tap; a promise is never closed for the agent. The closing toast says the whole result once, and its Undo says what it takes back ("Undo stage change"). Counted taps, every tap counted: Call, outcome, the one button = 3; Log, outcome, the one button = 3 for a call made outside the app (4 with another follow-up day). A text from a card is sent after 2 taps (Text, Send); the stage step then shows, and being back on Today is a 3rd tap. [core, web]
- **R-TODAY-8** **Plain words, no tutorial.** Every empty, loading and error state of this work says what is true in a sentence ("Nobody is due today. People you promise to follow up with will show here."), the group says what it is under its heading, and nothing opens by itself. [web]
- **R-TODAY-9** **Log a call or conversation from any screen, once.** Every page's header carries one entry beside the "+" (which stays a one-tap link to Add person): pick a person (the viewer's own recent people, R-TODAY-18, or search), then the same Log sheet. Entry, person, outcome, the one button = 4 taps when the person is recent; otherwise 4 taps and typing their name. In the sheet the direction says what it was ("I called from my phone") and "when" is now unless the agent says otherwise; left at now, the log carries the instant of the save. The log is idempotent on its client `noteId`: a double tap, a retry and two requests at the same moment are one note (R-STG-6's pattern). A Transaction Coordinator, who may not log an activity, is not offered the entry. [api, web]
- **R-TODAY-10** **Phone first.** Every new control is at least 44 px; Log sits beside the card's primary action; the page ends above the tab bar and the safe area; a sheet's one button is in its pinned footer and the sheet ends where the on-screen keyboard begins; a 24-character stage label is shown whole. Checked in jsdom by the tap token and in Chromium's iPhone 13 emulation by measurement; not on a real iPhone. [web]
- **R-TODAY-11** **Nothing is created or closed unseen; one rule decides what a new follow-up replaces.** (Fix pass: H1, M2, M3.) A one-pass sheet marks an earlier task done only when ALL hold (core `parity/people decideTaskReplacement`, tested as a table): it is the card's own task; its kind is `follow_up` or `callback`; its deadline has passed (`nextPromise.deadlineAt`); and the new follow-up is later than it. Otherwise it stays open, and when it is a follow-up or callback the sheet says the person will have two follow-ups. What the one button will add and mark done is named in the sheet's PINNED footer, directly under the button and described-by it, for Log, Call and Text and every outcome ("Adds: Follow up with Paula, Thu, Nov 26, 9:00 AM. Marks done: Follow up with Paula (was due today 9:30 PM)."); the button itself names only the stage, so it stays one line at 390 px. Order of one tap: the log, the follow-up, the stage, and LAST the earlier task. On a partial save the sheet stays open and says in one sentence what was saved, what was not, and what could not be confirmed (`people-edit/partialSave.ts`: "Saved: … Not confirmed: … Not saved: … <reason>"); a lost answer is never worded "it wasn't saved"; the next tap does only what is left and doubles nothing; leaving the sheet then says what was saved, with the stage's Undo. [core, web]
- **R-TODAY-12** **A manual note is never contact.** (M6.) The quiet clock counts CONTACT with the person: a call, a text, an email, a chat message, a held appointment, or a call, text or email the agent logged from outside the app (their own report). A manual note is somebody's words about the person (a Team Lead's housekeeping note, the agent's own reminder): it may be the Last line, labelled as a note, and it never takes a quiet person out of the plan. Core `selectLastContact` answers both (`lastTouch`; `lastContact` / `lastContactAt` by `isContactCandidate`); the card carries `lastConversation` when the Last line is a newer note. [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-13** **"Nobody is due today." is said only of a complete read.** (M4.) The plan's candidates are read in pages (`FOLLOW_UP_PAGE` = 60, oldest system touch first) and each page is judged by the SAME latest human contact the plan words its reason from, so a person cannot hide behind people whose system clock is stale. A read goes on until 20 people are found, the candidates run out, or its bound is reached: `FOLLOW_UP_DEPTH_DEFAULT` = 2 pages, and `GET /feed?followUpDepth=N` up to `FOLLOW_UP_DEPTH_MAX` = 10 (anything else is 422). The answer says `checked` (people looked at) and `complete`; `totalIsMinimum` is its negation. Today says "Nobody is due today." only when the read was complete and found nobody; otherwise "Checked 120 of your people so far, and nobody is due yet. There are more to check." with "Look further" (4, 8, then 10 pages), and at the deepest read that this is as far as Today looks. An incomplete read is never "You're caught up". [api, web]
- **R-TODAY-14** **One instant, two consistent lines.** (M5.) The Due line of a quiet person and the Last line are worded from one story (core `quietStory`; sent as `quiet: { basis, at, attempt }`): what stands at the latest of a readable contact, an attempt that did not reach the person, a touch the system counted with nothing readable behind it, and (only without a readable contact) imported history. Due, by basis: `contact` and `touch_only` "<Stage>: no conversation for N days" (N counted from that instant); `undelivered` "<Stage>, but the last text was not delivered"; `failed_dial` "<Stage>, but the last call did not connect"; `imported` "<Stage>: only imported history, the latest N days ago"; `never` "<Stage>: no conversation yet". Last, from the same story (web `lastLine`): the conversation itself; "No conversation is recorded. The last text (Nov 10) was not delivered."; "No conversation is recorded for the contact counted Nov 10."; "Imported history only (latest entry Nov 1). No conversation in Realty OS yet."; and for `never` no Last line at all on a quiet row (one sentence, not two). Who is due is unchanged: the plan still counts from the latest of the system's touch, a readable contact and imported history. [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-15** **A due promise is never hidden.** (M7.) Among a person's tasks due today or earlier the row leads with the earliest due promise when there is one (`taskIds[0]`; the person is still ordered by their earliest deadline). Its reason is said in the promise's own time, its Next line and Done are the promise's, and the other due tasks are named in one line ("Also due: Follow up with Dana"; three named, the rest counted: `alsoDue`, `alsoDueMore`). The one-row-per-person rule removes a promise's card from "Next up" only when the row leads with that promise or names it. A one-pass sheet opened from the row names every due task that stays open. [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-16** **A card about a person the viewer cannot open says nothing about them.** (M8.) A follow-up card whose person's detail the viewer may not open (a pond member holding a task on a lead that went back to the pond) is sent undecorated: `why` and every line are null (no Due, Last or Next line, no Log), its call, text, email, reply and send-draft actions are removed, its "Can't reach them right now" reason is not said (the viewer cannot see the person's phone or email, so the read cannot prove it), and the lead is named as on the pond card (first name and last initial). The person's own page says what the viewer may do (claim). [api, web]
- **R-TODAY-17** **The history read is one index read per person and per kind.** (M9.) Migration 0110 adds `appointments_contact_held_idx`, `notes_logged_activity_idx` and `notes_imported_contact_idx` (all partial); the two attempt reads look only at the person's newest 50 rows. Proved by EXPLAIN (ANALYZE) of the statement the route sends, as the viewer, over 20,000 appointments and a person with 5,000 notes. The message and call reads walk the person's rows in index order until their first match (no bound of their own). [api, db]
- **R-TODAY-18** **"Recent people" is the viewer's own recent activity.** (M11.) `GET /activities/recent-people` (any signed-in member; at most 8): the people the caller most recently logged for, wrote a note about or called, newest first, and only people they may log for now (the same `can('activity.log')` decision as the log route, on a live record). It reads existing rows (notes by author: `notes_author_recent_idx`, 0110; calls by agent) and writes nothing; opening a person or the picker records nothing. Texts and emails the caller sent are not read (no index by sender). [api, web]
- **R-TODAY-19** **An all-day task is a day in its own zone.** (M10.) `nextPromise.dueZone` is the zone an all-day task's day is in (the task's, else the team's): the zone `overdue` was decided in. The web words the day in that zone ("today", "yesterday", "tomorrow", "Thu", "Nov 19"), never in the zone of the viewer's device, so a task due today in New York is "today" for a viewer in Los Angeles, Tokyo or Auckland. A timed task is an instant and is said in the viewer's own time. [core, api, web]
- **R-TODAY-20** **A log the route accepted is shown at once.** (L17.) The log route accepts an activity dated up to 5 minutes ahead (a fast clock). Such a log is the last touch and a contact from the moment it is saved (core `LOGGED_AHEAD_MS`, held equal to `ACTIVITY_MAX_AHEAD_MS` by a test); nothing else dated ahead of now is ever a contact. [core, api]
- **R-TODAY-21** **Words that cannot mislead.** (L16.) A promise card is headed "You promised this" and its Due line says only when ("10 min overdue"); a teammate's call, text or note names the teammate, or says "by a teammate" when the viewer cannot see the name, and never reads as the viewer's own; a toast that says several results names what its Undo takes back ("Undo stage change"); after a one-pass sheet closes, the row it was opened from is scrolled clear of the toast. [web]

## 10. Phase 2-3 edge cases

E162–E200 belong to FUB parity (Part 3 §14; E194–E200: the People filters). The D2 cases (Gmail inbox sync and publishing) are E201–E229. E230–E233 (the FUB migration owner decisions of 2026-11-11) are in Part 3 §14 too. E283 (an Admin turns call reviews off, owner decision 2026-11-11) is listed with the coaching cases. The D5 cases are E290–E309 (E290–E299 photo and video texts, E300–E309 reactions). E310–E312 (owner decision D8 of 2026-11-12: a person's reply in the website chat is first contact, R-OWN-14) and E341–E343 (owner decision D8 of 2026-11-12: a new inquiry from a person an import left unassigned is routed, R-OWN-20) follow them; E313–E340, E352–E354 and E590–E597 (module 22's lead intake: the Lead sources page, Google Ads lead forms, tracking numbers, portal email forwarding; R-LSRC, R-GADS, R-TRK, R-LEM-9..12), E380–E389 (the telephony safeguards; R-TWL), E360–E377, E600–E619 and E620–E639 (owner decision D9 of 2026-11-12: the in-house CMA; E600–E619 its V2-A sharing, E620–E639 lane V2-B's in-house home values and seller value ranges), E400–E419 (module 42, documents: R-DOC-5…10), E420–E439 (module 48, voice answering API: R-VOICE-11…24), E440–E459 (module 52, Google Ads: R-ADS-7…13), E460–E474 (modules 53 and 54, Google Business Profile and coaching trends: R-GBP-3…5, R-COACH-8), E475–E489 (pilot deploy readiness: database roles, TLS and the app VM; R-DEPLOY-1..7), E490–E539 (owner decision D12, proposed: the AI ISA, R-ISA-*; module 75 and the core and seller lanes), E540–E559 (the backlog-gaps lane: listings on member removal, the merge guard, merged work, home-value retention, reactions in the merge window, the reminder race, honest-UI fixes), E560–E589 (owner decision D10: the homeowner profile, R-HOME-*), E598–E599 with E640–E653 (task 55: MLS size is finished living area only when the MLS confirms its field, and PostalCode as sent; R-MLS-7; numbered E598–E599 and E640–E653 on its branch, E600–E613 renumbered at batch 13 because E600–E639 are the CMA V2 lanes') and E660–E679 (module 55, the Phase 3 staff screens: R-STAFF-1…10) close this table. E680–E699 are module 45, the client portal API (R-PORTAL-8…19). E700–E719 are module 46, the client portal web (R-PORTALWEB-1…11; the brief named E680–E699, taken by module 45 as built, so they are renumbered here in the same order).

| # | Case | Handling | Rules |
|---|---|---|---|
| E71 | Facebook DM from someone unknown | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): Messenger/Instagram DMs are out of scope; nothing is received from Messenger.** Old handling: Intake creates "Unknown (Messenger)" with the connection's lead source, routes it; the 60-s reply goes out in Messenger (step channel `inbound_channel`, part of the approved content) | R-INBOX-1, R-INBOX-4 |
| E72 | Agent replies on Messenger 25 h after the client's last message | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger replies and no platform window are built.** Old handling: `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` before any provider call; compose offered SMS/email if allowed | R-INBOX-6 |
| E73 | Lead texted STOP last week, now writes on Instagram | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Instagram inbound exists.** Old handling: SMS stays revoked; Instagram is a separate channel and the person wrote first, so a person can answer there; automation is paused by the inbound | R-CHAN-4, R-INBOX-2 |
| E74 | "Please stop contacting me" arrives on Messenger | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger inbound exists; the same language by email, web chat or synced Gmail is E155/E213.** Old handling: Person-level do-not-contact + every channel on every point revoked (`needs_review`); queued sends canceled; the handback says what happened | R-CHAN-3, R-CHAN-2 |
| E75 | Messenger profile name matches an existing contact | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger identities are created.** Old handling: Never merged automatically; suggestion card; merge through R-OWN-24 | R-INBOX-1 |
| E76 | The same person messages two of the team's Facebook Pages; STOP on one | Two identities on one contact; the DB guard refuses DMs to both. D2: no DMs exist; the 0018 guard branch and its smoke check stay until the cleanup | R-INBOX-3, R-CHAN-4 |
| E77 | Chat visitor closes the tab mid-conversation | Session idle 30 min ⇒ ended; chat sends to the closed session are denied `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`; nothing automated goes to the phone or email the visitor typed; the lead card shows it as "typed in chat, not verified" and the agent may add it and call or text. A reply being written when the chat ends is never moved to another channel silently: the box says the chat ended, that the text is still there and where Send goes now, seen and announced (batch 6 review) | R-INBOX-5 |
| E78 | Zillow changes its lead email template | Unparsed fields stay empty; `needs_review` (`PARTIAL_FORMAT`/`UNKNOWN_FORMAT`) with the email encrypted; nothing routes until an Owner/Admin/Team Lead accepts it (the reviewer may type a missing phone/email). 2026-11-11 lead-email slice: an unknown or partial format is always held, even with a phone found (owner requirement) | R-INBOX-9, R-LEM-5 |
| E79 | Same lead arrives by email parse and partner feed | Dedup by `(source, external_id)` or the 10-minute fingerprint | R-OWN-25, R-INBOX-9 |
| E80 | A DM written before the guard knows the channel | Impossible: the channel CHECK and the guard map changed in one migration; an unknown channel cannot be stored, and the guard raises `CHANNEL_UNKNOWN`. D2: applies to every channel; no DM channel is offered | R-GATE-13 |
| E81 | MLS feed contains the team's own listing | Linked by `team_listing_id`; team row authoritative; status mismatch shown; counted once | R-MLS-5 |
| E82 | MLS listing withdrawn while its alert is scheduled | Send-time recheck ⇒ `skipped_ineligible` | R-NURT-4 |
| E83 | Price drops, then rises inside the digest window | The message reflects the current price; no net drop ⇒ skipped | R-NURT-3, R-NURT-4 |
| E84 | Five saved homes drop price the same day | One digested message listing five homes | R-NURT-3 |
| E85 | MLS feed down for three days | Connection `degraded`; seller updates say "MLS data as of …"; MLS-sourced alerts and firings pause | R-MLS-3 |
| E86 | Listing broker opts a listing out of internet display | Never shown to clients (alerts, updates, portal); pending alerts canceled | R-MLS-2 |
| E87 | A week with zero showings logged | The update says "No showings logged in Realty OS this week"; the agent may skip it | R-SELL-2 |
| E88 | Agent taps Send without a recommendation | `SELLER_UPDATE_NEEDS_RECOMMENDATION`; the DB refuses an approval without `edited_payload.recommendation` | R-SELL-3, R-SELL-4 |
| E89 | Buyer-agent feedback is rude or names a buyer | Shown verbatim to the agent with a remove control; never auto-sent or rule-published | R-SELL-2, R-PORTAL-3 |
| E90 | Price changes after the website page was approved | The page shows the new price (bound fact); no re-approval; prose unchanged | R-MKT-3 |
| E91 | Listing withdrawn with invites unsent | Invites canceled; page 410; exports flagged | R-MKT-9, R-MKT-7 |
| E92 | Pack requires the brokerage name at least as prominent as the team name and the flyer fails | Cannot approve; the reason is shown | R-MKT-1 |
| E93 | Draft says "perfect for young families" | Hard match ⇒ approval blocked until edited; "family room" is not flagged | R-FH-1, R-FH-5 |
| E94 | An invitee unsubscribes between approval and send | That recipient is blocked; "Sent 41 of 45; 4 opted out"; no `failed_send` items | R-MKT-8 |
| E95 | Template puts the inspection deadline on a holiday | Proposed date shown with the rule used; never silently moved; a person confirms | R-TXN-1, R-TXN-6 |
| E96 | Closing date moved by amendment | `due_version` bumps; firings and drafts citing the old version go stale; one update with the new date | R-TXN-5, R-TXN-4 |
| E97 | Lender's email is unsubscribed | Lender update blocked; `failed_send` to the deal owner/TC | R-TXN-4, R-GATE-1 |
| E98 | AVM value 30 % below last month | Jump check ⇒ draft for the agent | R-AVM-2 |
| E99 | AVM has no value for a condo | `skipped_ineligible` with "No estimate available"; never "unchanged" | R-AVM-2 |
| E100 | Rates move 30 bp | **Retired: rate moves are deferred (R-NURT-6).** When they land: one feed card per agent citing the series, a draft per contact only on tap | R-NURT-6 |
| E101 | Home anniversary on Feb 29 | Fires Feb 28 in common years | R-NURT-2 |
| E102 | Agent revokes the calendar app in Google | Link `revoked`; in-app appointments unaffected; reconnect card | R-CAL-4 |
| E103 | Agent edits the synced event in Google | Detected at the next push of that appointment (etag mismatch); detached, never overwritten; "Edited in Google; times may differ" | R-CAL-2 |
| E104 | Imported FUB recording | Never reviewed | R-COACH-1 |
| E105 | A rubric quote fails verification | Item `needs_review`; excluded from the denominator | R-COACH-2 |
| E106 | Agent has two scored calls in 14 days | No daily fix ("Not enough calls yet") | R-COACH-4 |
| E283 | An Admin turns call reviews off (owner decision 2026-11-11: Owner on, Admin off, as Gmail sync, except that the system cannot turn call reviews off and an Admin's off needs no re-auth) | Allowed without re-auth: open tips close at once, audited like the Owner's change. Turning them on, Team Lead visibility and the checklist stay the Owner's: an Admin's request that names any of them (even as stored), alone or together with turning reviews off, is refused whole (`OWNER_REQUIRED`; DB `OWNER_ONLY_SETTING`). The off sticks: each request names only the settings it changes and is decided on the settings row as it is (locked), so an Owner's checklist or Team Lead save from a page loaded earlier leaves reviews off, and turning them back on asks for the Owner's password against the row as it is | R-COACH-1, R-COACH-3 |
| E107 | Closed deal without commission | Excluded from dollars, listed as "missing commission" | R-OWNER-1 |
| E108 | A source with cost and zero closings | "— (0 closings)", never $0 or ∞ | R-OWNER-2 |
| E109 | Fewer than 10 deals entered a stage in the last year | That stage is left out of the forecast and counted ("not enough history") | R-OWNER-3 |
| E110 | Agent has no goals | The plan is built from the pipeline only and says so | R-PLAN-1 |
| E111 | The plan cites a deal reassigned since | Item dropped at render | R-PLAN-3 |
| E112 | After-hours call from a number that asked not to be called | Answered (they called); no callback offered by phone; confirmation only on an allowed channel | R-VOICE-6, R-GATE-14 |
| E113 | Caller asks "Am I talking to a real person?" | The scripted truthful answer | R-VOICE-8 |
| E114 | Caller asks what the seller would take | Declined politely; message taken; money handback | R-VOICE-5 |
| E115 | The contact's owner does not answer within the 20 s hold | Back to the caller; message taken or booked; critical handback and a promise task | R-VOICE-6, R-VOICE-7 |
| E116 | Caller in an all-party recording state | Disclosure + announcement before any processing; decline ⇒ voicemail | R-VOICE-2 |
| E117 | Voice vendor outage | Voicemail fallback | R-VOICE-10 |
| E118 | Two calls at once from the same unknown number | Address lock ⇒ one contact | R-OWN-25 |
| E119 | Caller speaks a language the playbook does not cover | Takes a short message or goes to voicemail, and says so | R-VOICE-5 |
| E120 | Contact has a self-reported PCS timeline | Not representable in the prediction feature type (compile-time test) | R-PRED-1 |
| E121 | Contact flagged "likely" | Feed card only; no message is sent because of it | R-PRED-4 |
| E122 | Public record owner name differs from the contact | Not attached; needs review | R-PRED-2 |
| E123 | Reallocation proposed while at the cap | Moves money between campaigns only; the tenant-wide total stays ≤ the ceiling in force | R-ADS-2 |
| E124 | Owner lowers the cap below month-to-date spend | Reconcile pauses all campaigns for the month (still a tap to resume); nothing retroactive; plans citing the old version are refused | R-ADS-5, R-ADS-1, R-ADS-2 |
| E125 | Grant revoked while the client is browsing | The next query returns nothing | R-PORTAL-6 |
| E126 | Client edits a URL to another deal's item | 404 (RLS) | R-PORTAL-2 |
| E127 | Spouses share one email | One account per contact; each grant explicit; the login link goes to the account's own point | R-PORTAL-2, R-PORTAL-5 |
| E128 | Agent retracts feedback already published | Item retracted; disappears on the next query; the retraction is final | R-PORTAL-3 |
| E129 | A client context queries any staff table or view | 0 rows from every table outside the allowlist (generated sweep) | R-PORTAL-1 |
| E130 | A file with malware is uploaded | Quarantined; never streamed, extracted or shared (module 42: the scan blocks it as `infected` and shreds its object, `documents/scan.ts`; the EICAR test file in `documents/documents.test.ts`) | R-DOC-3, R-DOC-7 |
| E131 | Extraction quotes a closing date that is not in the PDF | Needs review; never a milestone until confirmed (module 42: `documents/decide.ts` verifies the quote at its page's character range; the confirm is `EXTRACTION_CLAIM_NEEDS_REVIEW` and 0069 `app.deal_dates_evidence_guard` refuses the date) | R-DOC-2, R-DOC-8, R-TXN-1 |
| E132 | A Google review names the client and the price | The reply draft omits both; still a tap | R-GBP-1 |
| E133 | Negative review | Reply drafted on tap only; review requests were never gated by sentiment | R-GBP-1, R-MKT-11 |
| E134 | A Page is disconnected and reconnected in the same tenant | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger/Instagram identities exist; a Page is a publishing connection only.** Old handling: Same HMACs: identities, history and opt-outs intact; sends were `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED` meanwhile | R-CHAN-1, R-INBOX-7 |
| E135 | A Page moves from tenant A to tenant B | Live in one tenant at a time; B connects it only through the provider callback (proof of control); A's opt-outs stay in A (R-CONSENT-20); in B the window lets the team reply only to people who write first; the connect dialog says so. D2: a Page is a publishing connection only (R-MKT-12); the window part is gone (R-INBOX-6 retired), the one-tenant rule stands | R-INT-3 |
| E136 | Meta webhook for a Page with no connected or degraded connection | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): the product receives no Meta webhooks.** Old handling: `unrouted`, platform alert, nothing delivered | R-TEN-11 |
| E137 | 5,000 chat messages from one IP in a minute | Rate limits then 429; no contact, offer or notification | R-INBOX-5 |
| E138 | A chat visitor types an email matching an owned contact | Never attached: a new contact with only the chat identity, plus a duplicate suggestion "typed in chat, not verified" to the owner; the visitor sees only this session's replies; merging is a human decision (R-OWN-24). Merged as suggested (the earlier record survives), the chat moves with the person: the survivor answers it while it is open, and the merged-away record shows no chat (batch 6 review) | R-INBOX-1, R-INBOX-5, R-TEN-12 |
| E139 | A person-level do-not-contact contact DMs the Page | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no DMs are received; a do-not-contact contact who writes by email, web chat or synced Gmail is handled the same way.** Old handling: Inbound stored, pause, handback; every reply blocked `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`; the thread says so | R-CHAN-2 |
| E140 | A Messenger-only contact merges into an SMS contact; one side is do-not-contact | The survivor keeps the earliest `do_not_contact_at` (DB trigger, along merge chains); identities move; pause and opt-outs win; an address added to the survivor later is blocked too. D2: the same holds for a web-chat-only contact; no Messenger-only contact is created. Its chat sessions move with the identity (batch 6 review), so the survivor's summary says the chat is there and, with the do-not-contact, blocked | R-CHAN-2, R-OWN-24 |
| E141 | The MLS sends a modification older than the stored one | Refused (`MLS_STALE_UPDATE`); the sync skips it | R-MLS-1 |
| E142 | A TC edits a date while the agent drafts a client update citing it | `due_version` bumps; approving the draft returns `DRAFT_STALE` | R-TXN-5, R-TXN-4 |
| E143 | A deal spans a DST change | `due_at` is the stored end of day in the tenant zone, computed at confirm time | R-TXN-2, R-OWN-27 |
| E144 | Counsel withdraws approval of a prediction model | Scores computed with it, and their feed cards, are hidden at once | R-PRED-5 |
| E145 | The allocation job runs twice for one day | `(run, campaign, event_kind)` unique and `(approval, campaign)` unique for plans; the second run is refused | R-ADS-3, R-ADS-2 |
| E146 | The ad platform rejects the housing category or targeting | Campaign `rejected` with the platform's reason; no fallback targeting | R-ADS-4 |
| E147 | A co-buyer is removed from the deal | Their grant stops matching a party at once; their next request sees nothing | R-PORTAL-6 |
| E148 | A staff cookie is sent to `/api/portal`, or the reverse | 401; resolvers and cookies are disjoint | R-PORTAL-4 |
| E149 | A staff session sets the portal account GUC | `portal_account_id()` is NULL for staff; staff RLS only | R-TEN-14 |
| E150 | Voice answering turned off, or its playbook revoked, mid-evening | New AI-handled calls are refused by the DB; calls in progress finish with their handback; an Admin cannot turn it back on, even by re-activating the older policy version | R-VOICE-9, R-VOICE-7 |
| E151 | The assistant books a slot the agent just booked in-app | The booking transaction takes the user's advisory lock and re-reads free slots; the assistant offers the next slot | R-VOICE-4 |
| E152 | An open-house email to 900 contacts, 40 unsubscribed | 860 sent, 40 blocked and counted on the send, not 40 exceptions | R-MKT-8 |
| E153 | A forged "Zillow lead" email from an unaligned domain | `needs_review`; no routing to an autonomous first response; nothing is texted to the number inside it | R-INBOX-9 |
| E154 | A chat visitor types someone else's phone number | Nothing automated is sent to it; the agent sees it marked "typed in chat, not verified" | R-INBOX-5 |
| E155 | A chat visitor writes "stop contacting me" | Person-level do-not-contact on the session's own contact only; a later merge into an existing contact shows the do-not-contact on the merge screen and carries it. Every channel of the person is said blocked before the tap ("Asked not to be contacted at all"), texts and email the agent added included, not only the chat (batch 6 review) | R-CHAN-3, R-CHAN-2 |
| E156 | A confirmed inspection deadline passes uncompleted | `milestone_at_risk` (overdue) for the deal owner, in the TL queue within 60 s | R-ACC-6, R-TXN-3 |
| E157 | A confirmed appraisal date is 36 h away with no responsible party | `milestone_at_risk` (unassigned) | R-ACC-6 |
| E158 | A proposed (unconfirmed) date is tomorrow | Never a risk and never reminded until a person confirms it | R-TXN-2, R-TXN-3 |
| E159 | A wet-signed contract is scanned to an image-only PDF | "No text found in this PDF; enter the dates by hand"; no proposals (module 42: the extraction's `no_text` outcome, `documents/extract.ts`) | R-DOC-2, R-DOC-8 |
| E160 | A tenant tries to connect a Page it does not manage | The callback's proof fails; nothing is linked; the row stays unconfigured. D2: also for a Facebook Page or Instagram business account connected for publishing | R-INT-3 |
| E161 | An Owner approves a shift; the job replays the approval the next day | The second planned row is refused (approval single-use) | R-ADS-2 |
| E201 | A client's Reply-All reaches both platform inbound and the agent's Gmail | Stored and acted on once when the platform row carries the Message-ID hash: the platform row wins; the Gmail copy is refused, or deleted when the platform row lands after it. One handback: both paths use the dedupe key `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the Message-ID hash is known, so whichever lands first hands back and the other does not. A platform row without the hash is not deduplicated, so a backfill can show that message twice: a row saved before module 22 writes the hash (the `platform:gmail` rollout precondition waits such rows out of the backfill window, or a one-time repair hashes them) and imported email (never hashed) | R-GMAIL-12, R-GMAIL-10 |
| E202 | A client writes from an address not on file | Not stored; the agent may add the address to the contact by hand and confirm it (one-tap suggestion), and later mail syncs (earlier mail only through "import again", inside the window) | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E203 | The agent's personal mail (family, doctor, bank) | Never stored: only addresses the agent confirmed for sync (`mail_sync_points`) are matched, so a peer who adds a stranger's address to a contact and makes the agent a collaborator or deal member, or a TL who adds the agent's own mailbox as a contact point, captures nothing; a live mailbox address of the team is never a client address (`MAIL_ADDRESS_IS_A_MAILBOX`) | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E204 | A client is Cc'd on a thread with a lender who is not a contact | Stored for the client (role `cc`); the lender's address is never stored ("and 1 other") | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E205 | The agent Bcc's a client | Stored with role `bcc`; the other recipients only as a count | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E206 | The agent forwards a client's email to a personal friend | No contact address on the forward ⇒ not stored | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E207 | A client forwards a third party's email | Stored as the client's message; quoted history stripped best effort | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E208 | A contract PDF is attached | Metadata only (name, type, size), never fetched; the agent uploads it to documents if wanted | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E209 | A 500-message thread | At most `max_messages_per_thread` copies; the timeline says older messages stay in Gmail | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E210 | A shared mailbox a teammate already linked | Refused (one live link per mailbox) | R-GMAIL-2 |
| E211 | The same Gmail connected in a second workspace | Refused (one live link per mailbox across all tenants) | R-GMAIL-2 |
| E212 | The agent removes a thread | Hidden at once, deleted within one sync interval, later messages never stored; no un-exclude | R-GMAIL-7 |
| E213 | "Please stop emailing me" sent to the agent's Gmail | Person-level do-not-contact (`needs_review`), the same as platform email | R-GMAIL-9 |
| E214 | An old "unsubscribe" inside the backfill | Revokes email on that point | R-GMAIL-9 |
| E215 | The agent leaves the team | Removed: link revoked, token deleted, copies hidden at once and deleted (owner question Q-GMAIL-1, default delete). Suspended: the link is paused, not revoked; copies hidden until reactivation, then sync resumes | R-GMAIL-13, R-INT-5 |
| E216 | A contact is reassigned A → B | B sees A's earlier synced mail like the timeline; A's new mail with that contact is no longer stored | R-GMAIL-6, R-GMAIL-5 |
| E217 | The agent revokes access in their Google account | `invalid_grant` ⇒ `PROVIDER_REVOKED`, handled as a disconnect | R-GMAIL-13 |
| E218 | The stored history id has expired | Bounded resync inside the backfill window; never a full-mailbox read; a live message it finds that is older than 1 hour is stored and opt-out-classified but neither pauses nor hands back | R-GMAIL-11, R-GMAIL-10 |
