Call, text or email them one to one.", Send goes away, and the agent's own text of the same words still goes as a one-to-one message; the one approved just before is blocked at dispatch and refused by the database (0100) (round 3 item 3, OQ-B18-S6); the AI does not answer (Phase 1 voicemail, "The assistant did not answer: …"), and `app.calls_source_guard` refuses a takeover marked after the ring; the person's own text, email and call go | R-LSRC-8 |
| E728 | A referral partner's people come in from Follow Up Boss or a CSV file (b18 round 3 item 1, R-LSRC-8): each carries the partner's source label; one is a person already in the CRM whom the import matches by phone; the Owner maps the label to the source marked "No automated contact for leads from this source" at import time (FUB) or afterwards (CSV), while one of them is already in a plan; an Admin tries to map it; another label is mapped to a source without the setting | The import's preview (`GET /imports/:id/parity`) and summary (`GET /imports/:id/report`) list every label with its people, the source it means and whether that source is marked; the Admin's change is refused 404 and the database refuses it (`LEAD_SOURCE_IMPORT_LABELS_OWNER_ONLY`); once mapped, `app.person_from_lead_source` counts the label's people (a person the import created, and a person already here onto whom the import committed the matched record; a person a file only matched, whose record was not committed, is not covered: E755) as the source's: no first response, no plan by hand or the database, no stage plan, bulk enroll counted, a queued alert blocked at dispatch, and the running plan stops; the other label's people are not covered; the person's own one-to-one text goes; unmapping (re-auth) takes them off the marked source and leaves them in review, refused for the unclassified reason until the Owner maps the label again (R-LSRC-9, E732) | R-LSRC-8 |
| E729 | A person came in from an import and nobody has reviewed their source (R-LSRC-9, owner decision 2026-11-14): an unmapped label, a label two lead sources hold, or no label at all; then every automated path meets them: the first response, a plan by hand, a stage entry, a closed deal, a bulk enroll and a bulk email (before the draft and after it), a module 26 alert, an open-house invite, a queued automated text and a saved-search alert, AI care, an AI-answered call, and a tap on a draft a plan or an alert wrote | Each refuses exactly where a marked source's person is refused, with the code `SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED` (AI care: `AI_ISA_SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED`; the stage skip and the 409's `details.reason`: `source_unclassified`; an invite: outcome `source_unclassified`) and its own sentence: the person came in from an import, their imported source has not been reviewed yet, an Owner reviews it under Lead sources. Never "is on for this person's lead source" and never the setting's name. When a marked source also sent them, that reason wins. An invite asks only about people the sender can see, so an unseen record never reveals that it is imported or unreviewed | R-LSRC-9 |
| E730 | A plan or AI care is already running when the person becomes unclassified, or is paused and someone resumes it | The plan stops the next time its step job runs, due or not (`stop_reason source_unclassified`, unsent steps canceled with that reason, nothing sent); a paused plan cannot be resumed (`POST /enrollments/:id/resume` 409 `SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED`; "Resume automation" answers 200, lifts only the conversation pause and says "1 plan was not resumed: this person came in from an import and their imported source has not been reviewed yet (an Owner reviews it under Lead sources). …"; the database refuses the row). AI care pauses `source_unclassified` at its next turn with an honest note, and cannot be resumed or continued (409 `AI_ISA_SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED`, the database too); it can still be ended | R-LSRC-9 |
| E731 | A person on the team calls, texts or emails an unclassified person; that person texts STOP or HELP, or asks for their own portal sign-in link | The one-to-one call, text and email go. The STOP confirmation, the HELP reply and the sign-in link go: exactly the exemptions of the marked-source rule (`opt_out_confirmation`, `help_reply`, `portal_login_link`), in dispatch and in the message guard, and no wider: any other system message to them is refused | R-LSRC-9, R-LSRC-8 |
| E732 | The Owner removes a label's mapping while that label's people have a running plan (its next step a day away), live AI care and a pending draft a plan step wrote; another label's people have the same | In the Owner's request: the label's running plans are asked for a step now (which stops them `source_unclassified`), its live AI care for a turn now (which pauses it) and its pending drafts are withdrawn with the reason and their cards closed; the answer counts them (`stopping`, `pausing`, `withdrawn`) and the audit row (`LEAD_SOURCE_IMPORT_LABEL_UNMAPPED`) carries who, when, the sources, the label and its digest (R-LSRC-10) and the counts. The row reads "not reviewed yet …" (`inReview`). Nobody of another label is touched. Bounded work, no lock | R-LSRC-9 |
| E733 | A wrong pick: the Owner maps a label to a marked source, then moves it to an unmarked one, then back; a draft was withdrawn while the person was in review and the Owner then reviews the source | The people follow the current mapping in both directions: marked, they are refused `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT` and a running plan stops for that reason; moved to an unmarked source the refusal lifts and a plan can start; the list says so after every change. Nothing is restored: a stopped plan stays stopped, and a late tap on the withdrawn draft answers 409 with "… was withdrawn while this person's imported source had not been reviewed yet. Nothing was sent. Write to them yourself." | R-LSRC-9 |
| E734 | People came in with no source label at all (a file with no source column), in two different imports | Each import's no-label group is one row, classified by the Owner like a label (`PUT /lead-sources/import-labels { unlabeledImportId, leadSourceId }`; an Admin gets 404; another tenant's or an unknown import 404; a blank `label` 422). Classified to an unmarked source the refusal lifts for THAT import's people only; removed, their running plans stop; classified to a marked source they are refused for that reason. One import's group never classifies another's | R-LSRC-9 |
| E735 | An import's dry run matches a person already in the CRM who has a running plan and a pending draft; later the import is committed and attaches the record to them; and a database that already holds imported people is upgraded to 0101 | The dry run changes nothing for anyone, whatever the label is mapped to (a marked source included since 0103: E755): no refusal, the plan runs, the draft waits, no audit row. The commit makes the person unclassified (unless their label was already mapped) and, in the commit chunk's own transaction, asks their plan for a step (it stops), their AI care for a turn and withdraws their drafts, counted in an audit row (`CONTACTS_COMMIT_SOURCE_NOT_REVIEWED_STOPPED`); a person whose label the Owner mapped beforehand keeps their plan. The upgrade changes no plan, message, draft or import row; every already-imported person without an Owner-mapped label reads as unclassified from that moment | R-LSRC-9 |
| E736 | A row is written straight to the database, bypassing the API: an automated message inserted in a sending state (dispatching, sent, delivered, dispatch_unknown) or moved to dispatching, a plan inserted active or made active again, a call inserted as the assistant's, AI care inserted or resumed, for an unclassified or a marked source's person | Each guard refuses it with the reason that applies (`SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED: …` or `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT: …`; AI care `AI_ISA_SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED: …`). A queued row, a paused insert, a pause, a stop, a person's own message or call, an inbound call, imported history, and the status reconciliation of a message already on its way pass. The guards read the row first and take no lock. With no tenant context they return, as before (only the migration owner writes that way; no role that bypasses row-level security can make an activating write: §21.68) | R-LSRC-9 |
| E737 | An inbound STOP, an inbound call, a pause and a stop arrive while an Owner's mapping removal, or an import commit chunk, is still open; a hand enrolment, or a dispatch, starts just before a mapping removal commits | The four succeed at once for the very people the mapping touches, and for everyone an import chunk is not writing; a person an open chunk is itself attaching a record to is held by the import's own contact lock, as before, so their STOP is recorded when the chunk commits (it waits, it never fails). The enrolment is accepted (it read the mapping as it was) but its first step is refused: the step runner stops the plan and nothing is sent. The dispatch whose TX1 committed before the removal sends that one message; the plan's next step and every later automated message are refused | R-LSRC-9 |
| E738 | A file's source cell, or a Follow Up Boss source, has spaces around it, capitals, an un-normalized accent, an emoji that straddles the older cut at 500 units, or is longer than every older cut; another is only whitespace; another holds a NUL or half of a surrogate pair, or is longer than 32,768 characters | The label is kept character for character on the import record, on the person, in the mapping and on every review row; each spelling is its own row. Only whitespace is no label (the person belongs to the import's no-label group). A label that cannot be stored exactly rejects its record (`SOURCE_LABEL_UNREADABLE`; a file holding a NUL is refused whole, `CSV_BINARY`): it is never altered or cut to fit, and a rejected record blocks nobody. The database keeps a review row's label fixed and unique per team by its whole text | R-LSRC-10 |
| E739 | An import commits people under a label for the first time; a later import commits the same label; the Owner had mapped the label ahead of any commit | The review record says where it was first seen: that first import, its kind and when; a later import leaves it alone; a label mapped ahead gets its first-seen at its first commit. Only the system (an import's commit) can write it, once; the Owner and an Admin cannot | R-LSRC-10 |
| E740 | Someone other than the Owner writes a mapping record straight to the database, or the Owner records one in another person's name or with no time | Refused (`LEAD_SOURCE_IMPORT_LABELS_OWNER_ONLY`): an Admin, the system, an agent and a team lead cannot (the last two do not see the table at all); another team's lead source cannot be named; nobody deletes a review record | R-LSRC-10 |
| E741 | A label reads the same as a lead source's name, or as a label the Owner already mapped, apart from capitals and outer spaces; another reads the same as two lead sources; the Owner confirms a suggestion, confirms a different source, or confirms after the sources changed | The first is `suggested` with that source and says so; it is NOT mapped: its people stay in review until the Owner confirms exactly that suggestion (anyone else 404). The second is `ambiguous`: nothing is suggested and the Owner maps it by choosing. A confirmation of anything but the current suggestion is `409 IMPORT_LABEL_SUGGESTION_CHANGED` with what is true now, and nothing is mapped. Confirming a look-alike label maps only that label. The preview, and reading any list, map nothing | R-LSRC-10 |
| E742 | The Owner maps a label, moves it to another source and removes the mapping | Each change writes an audit row with the label, its digest, the review record, the old and the new source, the Owner and the time, and what was asked to stop (`…_MAPPED`, `…_REMAPPED`, `…_UNMAPPED`; a confirmation `…_SUGGESTION_CONFIRMED`); the review row shows who changed it last and when. Asking for the mapping a label already has changes nothing and writes no row. A lead source that already holds 200 imported labels (or 200 imports' no-label groups) refuses one more with a plain 422 (`lead_source_label_limit`) and changes nothing: the Owner maps it to another lead source, so nobody is left with no way to be classified | R-LSRC-10, R-AUDIT-4 |
| E743 | A label goes from in review to an unmarked source, to a marked source, back to in review and back to the unmarked source | After every change the list, the import's summary and the answer say the same thing, and the people follow at once in both directions: open, refused `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT`, refused `SOURCE_UNCLASSIFIED`, open again. A person the import only matched under the label, whose record no import committed, is covered by neither reason (migration 0103, E755; until then the marked source covered them, 0099's reading) | R-LSRC-10, R-LSRC-9 |
| E744 | People came in with no source label; the Owner maps, moves and removes that group from the list | The group is one row (`label` '', `unlabeledImportId` set), mapped by its import, never by an empty label (422); nothing is suggested for it; each change is audited with the import (`LEAD_SOURCE_IMPORT_UNLABELED_MAPPED` / `_REMAPPED` / `_UNMAPPED`) and its people follow. The web's choice for that row asks by the import | R-LSRC-10 |
| E745 | A team holds 60,030 imported people and 160,030 import records; the Owner maps, confirms and lists; an import's commit meets a deadlock, a canceled statement, a lock it cannot take, a lost connection or a server out of resources while applying one record; a database that already holds committed labels is upgraded to 0102 | A mapping and a confirmation read only their own label's rows (tens of rows, tens of milliseconds); the list answers inside its stated time with every count right. The transient error fails the chunk as a whole and it runs again: every person is imported and no record is marked failed (an error of the record itself still rejects only that record). The upgrade writes one review record per committed label and no-label group and changes nothing else | R-LSRC-10, R-IMPORT-4 |
| E746 | Another team's Owner, agent or system actor asks for this team's labels, maps to this team's source, names this team's import as a no-label group, confirms with this team's source, or calls the source definers for a person of this team; an agent or a team lead of the same team opens the review; support looks at it | Another team reads and changes nothing: the list is its own, each write is 404, and `app.person_source_refusal`, `app.person_no_ai_care_source`, `app.person_from_lead_source` and `app.import_source_people` answer NULL, false, false and no rows whichever tenant id is passed. In the team, only Owner, Admin and the system read the review records; an agent and a team lead get 404 and read no row; support reads only with a live grant and never writes | R-LSRC-10, R-TEN-1, R-TEN-3, R-PERM-11 |
| E747 | A person nothing automatic may go to (a marked source's, or imported and unreviewed) has an active saved search with a new matching home; an alert was already queued when they became covered; several such people's searches sort before a reviewed person's | No alert is queued and no match or last-alert time is recorded for them; the queued alert is refused at dispatch with the reason that applies and its homes are released; they never fill the batch. Once the Owner reviews the source (or turns the setting off) the homes still on the market are alerted, and only then recorded. While they wait their searches are held, so the sender does not ask about them on every run (E756) | R-LSRC-10, R-AST-10 |
| E748 | The Owner opens an import (Follow Up Boss or a file) after its snapshot and runs it with a label and the no-label people left in review | "Review sources" sits above the categories before any dry run: each label exactly as imported, its people, its state in words, and the no-label people as one row. It says how much is still in review, that the import may run anyway, and that those people get no automated contact until an Owner chooses a lead source while the team can still call, text or email them one to one. The contacts category repeats it next to Approve and Import; nothing waits on it. After the import the report carries the same review once, counted by the people actually imported; the Lead sources page shows the same list | R-LSRC-10, R-LSRC-9 |
| E749 | The Owner changes a row's dropdown (or arrows through it) and presses nothing; then presses the button; later changes the mapping; then removes it, backing out once | Changing the dropdown sends nothing: the row says what applying the choice would do and offers "Map to …" (or "Change to …") and Cancel. Only the button sends the mapping, with the label exactly as received (the no-label row by its import, never an empty label). Removing asks first, says that the people go back in review, what stops, and that nothing restarts on its own; "Keep it" sends nothing. The answer's words are the row's new sentence and what was stopped "for these people" (a marked source: "its leads") | R-LSRC-10 |
| E750 | A label is shown as an exact match of a lead source; before the Owner confirms, that source is renamed or a second source comes to match | The dropdown was never pre-set to the suggestion. Confirm asks the server for exactly the source shown; the server works the suggestion out again and answers `409 IMPORT_LABEL_SUGGESTION_CHANGED`: nothing is mapped, the row says the suggestion changed and that nothing was changed, the list is read again, and the stale suggestion cannot be retried | R-LSRC-10 |
| E751 | The review cannot be loaded; a mapping fails (no connection); the Owner closes the password prompt; the chosen lead source already holds 200 labels; an Admin opens the review; the Owner has no lead source yet | Loading and "nothing imported yet" are said. A failed load is one error with Try again. A failed action stays on its row with what happened, "Nothing was changed." and Try again, which sends the same request (after the password, when the server asks for it); the row never reads as mapped meanwhile. The limit is a plain sentence with the way out (choose another lead source). An Admin reads the rows and who maps, with no dropdown and no button; an Owner with no lead source is told to add one and is offered no choice that cannot be made | R-LSRC-10, R-PERM-13 |
| E752 | A label differs from a lead source's name only in a way plain trimming and lowercasing do not remove: an accent stored composed in one and decomposed in the other, full-width letters, "ß" against "SS", a dotted capital I, or a zero-width space inside or around it | No suggestion: the comparison is JavaScript's `trim()` and locale-free `toLowerCase()` and nothing else (it is not normalized, folded or transliterated), so "CAFÉ TOUR" is suggested for "Café tour" and a no-break space around a label is trimmed, but none of the forms above match. The label stays in review until the Owner maps it by choosing (owner question OQ-B18-I9) | R-LSRC-10 |
| E753 | A text or email is submitted and the provider's answer never comes; ten minutes of asking the provider find nothing either way | The message ends `failed` with reason `DISPATCH_UNKNOWN_UNRESOLVED` (unchanged), but it was never proved undelivered. Not every sentence for it is reworded yet. Three surfaces are, and say that delivery could not be confirmed, that it may have reached them, and to check before sending again: the thread's line (while it is still being checked, and after), the compose notice after Send (a first answer and a re-tap, which both carry the message's status reason), and the System page's two reconcile sentences (neither says the provider has no record of it). On those three it is never "Not delivered", "It didn't send" or "Nothing reached them". Three surfaces are NOT reworded and still say "not sent" of a message that ended this way; they are queued for the next batch (OQ-B18-I12): (1) the seller update card, which words any failed delivery "Not sent: the provider failed" (the API sends that card no status reason); (2) an approval or a feed card's Send tapped again after the ten minutes: the replay answers the message's status without its reason, so the Approvals page says "It didn't send. The phone service refused it. Nothing reached them." and the feed card announces "Not sent." (while the answer is still being checked, `dispatch_unknown`, both say that delivery could not be confirmed); (3) the AI care views, whose verb for any failed message is "not sent": the handoff card's pending line reads "not sent" and the thread labels the message "Written by", as if it had never left. The message is never sent again automatically; a new send is a person's choice | R-GATE-8 |
| E754 | The telephony provider accepts a submit (HTTP success) but its answer cannot be read: not JSON, cut off, or with no usable message id | An unknown outcome, never a plain failure: the message becomes `dispatch_unknown` with no invented provider reference and one reconcile job, exactly as after a timeout. The same command posted again, a repeated dispatch and a re-tap submit nothing more; a later lookup or a signed status callback settles it without another submit; with no evidence after ten minutes it ends as E753. A read (not a submit) that cannot be parsed is still "unavailable". A deliberate new message from a person is a second submit: the app does not claim otherwise | R-GATE-8 |
| E755 | A label is mapped to a lead source marked "No automated contact for leads from this source" (or an import's no-label group is counted as one). A file carrying the label is uploaded and dry-run: its rows match three people already in the CRM, one of them in a running plan. The import is abandoned. In a second import a reviewer attaches two of the matched rows to their people and the import commits; the third row is left waiting. Separately, a token-authenticated intake caller sends `import_source` among its own source-detail keys | The dry run, the rows waiting for review and the abandoned import refuse nobody and stop nothing: the matched people read as before, a plan can be started for one by hand and the running plan runs on at its next step. The commit that attaches a record to a person (outcome `imported` or `merged`) is what makes them the marked source's lead: refused `SOURCE_NO_AUTOMATED_CONTACT` from then on, their running plan asked for a step in the commit's own transaction (it stops) and the stop audited. The person whose row was left waiting is untouched. A skipped, rejected or reverted row, a row of another import category and a key on the person's own record cover nobody. The CRM-wide review row counts the people imported, never the people matched, and its sentence ("nothing automatic reaches the people imported with it") is about exactly those. An import's OWN row and summary (`GET /imports/:id/parity`, `/report`) count the file's people, so for a label or a no-label group mapped to a marked source they never say that: they say the numbers and what each means, for example "3 people in this file. 2 were imported or attached and get no automated contact, because the label is mapped to (the source), which has "No automated contact for leads from this source" on. 1 was already in the CRM and is not imported from this file, so this mapping does not cover them: their row is not attached." (while the contacts are not imported yet it ends "unless their row is attached", and with nobody imported yet it says so and that the mapping covers a person only once their row is imported or attached). The row carries the third number as `matchedOnlyPeople` and the web shows the sentence as it is, with a warning chip on the row while anyone is not covered. A matched person an earlier import already committed under the same label is covered and is not counted there. Until review round 6 that row said "(3 people) ... nothing automatic reaches the people imported with it" while the third person, only matched, went on getting the plan's texts. Applying 0103 changes no row and restarts nothing: a plan the older reading already stopped stays stopped (OQ-B18-I13) | R-LSRC-8, R-LSRC-9 |
| E756 | A team has 5,003 people nothing automatic may go to (imported, their source in review), each with an active saved search and a matching home never alerted, three lead sources marked, and one reviewed person whose search sorts after all of them. The alert sender runs every ten minutes and once per newly matched listing. Later the Owner maps one small label; two holds run out; the Owner maps the big label | The first run alerts the reviewed person, asks the source check once per covered person (a page at a time, outside the due query, no lock on them), holds every covered search for an hour and records nothing as alerted for them. Every later run skips the held searches on a plain column and asks nothing about them (stated times: first run under 20 s, later runs under 1 s; measured about 8 s, which is one question per covered person, and under 50 ms; fewer than 50 index scans of `lead_inquiries` by two later runs, where the sender that called the definer in its due query made more than 10,000 and took the 8 s on every run). Every page is asked before any person is locked, so the first run's seconds are never seconds during which an alerted person's lock is held. Mapping the small label clears that label's holds and nobody else's, and the next run alerts its people; a hold that ran out is asked again once and, still covered, held again; mapping the big label clears 5,000 holds in one bounded statement. A hold never lets a message go | R-LSRC-10, R-AST-10 |
| E757 | A team has 8,000 people in a running plan and 3,000 pending plan drafts, none of them a referral partner's. The Owner maps a 10-person label (3 in a running plan, 1 with a pending draft) to a marked source, and turns the setting on for another source that sent 12 people four ways (its own records, one merged away since; an inquiry; a label; a no-label group) | Each request starts from the label's or the source's own people and joins them to what there is to stop: the right plans are asked for a step and stop for the marked source's reason (the merged-away lead's survivor included), the right draft is withdrawn, nobody else's plan or draft is touched, and the source check is asked once per person who has something to stop (stated time: under 2 s each; 8 index scans of `lead_inquiries` by the two requests, where asking about every plan, AI care and draft of the team made 22,016 and took about 5 s each, on the way to the 30 s statement timeout) | R-LSRC-8, R-LSRC-10 |
| E758 | A person imported under a label nobody has reviewed has an active saved search with a home waiting. The alert sender held the search an hour ago; the hold has run out. The sender's next run asks again, holds the search again and is still working: its transaction is open. Meanwhile the Owner maps that label to a lead source that has a tracking number; a client texts STOP to that tracking number, and someone calls it | The Owner's mapping answers at once and is committed: it clears only the holds it can take at once (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`) and skips the search the sender holds. The inbound STOP and the inbound call, which read that lead source FOR SHARE and so wait for the Owner's request, are answered at once too, and the STOP is recorded. Nothing is alerted meanwhile. The skipped search stays held, never longer than the hour; when the hold has run out the sender asks again and the person, now classified, gets the home that waited (an alert up to an hour late). Before this the mapping waited for the sender's whole run while holding the lead source's row, and the STOP and the call waited behind it (they did not fail) | R-LSRC-10, R-LSRC-9 |
| E759 | An agent leaves a voicemail for a new lead and logs it; later leaves another | The log answers `suggestedStage: attempting` ("You left a voicemail. That is a try, not a conversation yet."). The stage does not change until the agent confirms it with the stage route. A second voicemail to someone already in `attempting` suggests nothing; a voicemail never suggests `connected` | R-STG-2 |
| E760 | The person replies to a text, or the agent talks with them, while they are already `connected` | Nothing is suggested: a reply or a conversation never suggests `active`. Only the appointment result `agreement_signed` does | R-STG-2 |
| E761 | An agent logs an outside call "connected" on a routed lead they have not reached in the app yet | `suggestedStage: { stage: 'connected', locked: true }` and `alsoOffer: [{ stage: 'nurture', locked: true }]`: a logged outside activity is never a first contact (R-PAR-32), so the stage route would refuse both (`STAGE_NEEDS_CONTACT`). A voicemail's `attempting` is not locked. For a Team Lead the same suggestion is not locked | R-STG-2, R-PAR-14 |
| E762 | An agent previews moving a person who texted STOP into a stage whose approved plan texts | `wontStart: { reason: 'cannot_be_reached' }` with its sentence; `starts` is null. The change then writes a decision row with that same reason and enrolls nobody. The same holds for do-not-contact, a source marked "No automated contact", an unreviewed imported source, a draft-only plan, and live AI care | R-STG-3 |
| E763 | The person is already in a plan and the agent previews a stage whose plan is another one | `wontStart: { reason: 'already_in_a_plan', alreadyIn: { enrollmentId, plan } }` and the sentence names both: "Contract plan will not start: they are already in Buyer plan, and a person is in one plan at a time. Stop Buyer plan first if you want Contract plan." `keepsRunning` shows the running (or paused) plan and whether the viewer may stop it. A running plan the viewer may not see is "the owner's plan" in the field and the sentence | R-STG-3 |
| E764 | An agent changes a stage, a plan starts, and the agent taps Undo; or undoes a move away from a stage whose plan has ended | The stage goes back. The plan that started is still running: the answer says so (`stillRunning`) and stopping it is a separate, working step. Going back INTO a stage that has a plan starts nothing: the decision is skipped, reason `undo` (the same move without `undoOf` starts the plan). The worker decides a marked event the same way if the request could not | R-STG-5 |
| E765 | A request names, as `undoOf`, another person's stage event, another team's, an older one of this person, an unknown id, or the latest event with a stage it did not come from | 409 `STAGE_UNDO_INVALID`; the stage, the history, the decisions and the enrollments are unchanged. Set straight in the database, such a value is not kept: the history definer stores NULL and the change is an ordinary stage change. The system (an import) never marks an undo | R-STG-5 |
| E766 | The agent double-taps Save, refreshes, or the phone retries the request; two identical requests arrive at the same moment | One stage event and one decision. Each request answers 200 with the same `eventId` and the same `automation` (the stored decision), also when the retry carries the first request's row version. A retried log answers the same suggestion until the stage is confirmed, then none | R-STG-5, R-STG-2 |
| E767 | A stage is set by PATCH or a bulk action (decided by the worker within a minute), and the contact page asks what happened | `lastStageChange: { pending: true, decision: null }`: neither "started" nor "nothing happened". Once the worker decides, `pending` is false and the decision is shown. A change to a stage no plan names is not pending and has no decision | R-STG-4 |
| E768 | A plan's text is handed to the carrier and no answer comes; ten minutes of asking find nothing either way | `plan.deliveryUnconfirmed: { checking: true }` while the carrier is being asked, then `{ checking: false }`: "Delivery of the plan's last text could not be confirmed: it may have reached them. The plan did not submit it a second time. Check with them before sending it again yourself." (E753's words.) It is never called sent, delivered, not delivered or retried, and nothing here submits it a second time. While it is being checked it is not `next`; once unresolved the plan's next step is the one after it | R-STG-4, R-GATE-8 |
| E769 | A collaborator (another agent, an ISA) previews, changes or undoes the stage of a person whose owner has a private plan, or opens the status | The plan is named "the owner's plan" everywhere: the preview, the stage answer, `stillRunning`, the status and its sentences. They may pause or stop it (the routes allow a collaborator), and Resume is not offered because the resume route cannot find a plan they cannot see. The Owner, an Admin and a Team Lead are told the name | R-STG-3, R-STG-4, R-STG-5, R-PERM-12 |

| E771 | The Owner renames the stages, calling `nurture` "Active client", `active` "Not moving forward" and `lost` "New lead" | Only the words on screen change. | E772 | A person has a purchase under contract and a sale with an agreement signed; their own stage is moved through every key, "closed" and "lost" included | Both deals, their stage history, their dates and their people are unchanged after every move. | E773 | The agent picks a follow-up in the stage step and confirms; the task is saved and the stage change then fails; the sheet stays open | The sheet says "Follow-up saved for <date>", the date of the task `POST /tasks` answered, and the chips are locked. Tests: `apps/web/src/surfaces/people-edit/FollowUp.test.tsx` | R-STG-6 |
| E774 | After a saved follow-up the agent taps Change and picks another time, or None; or the first save's answer was lost and the agent picks another chip before retrying | Change applies each choice to the stored task at once through `PATCH /tasks/:id` (a new `dueAt`, or `status: canceled` for None); after a removal the next follow-up is a new task id. A replay that answers another date than the one now chosen (the first request was stored and its answer lost) is changed to the chosen date for real; if that is refused the sheet shows the date that is stored, stays open and does not change the stage. No date is ever on screen that the server did not answer. Tests: `FollowUp.test.tsx` | R-STG-6 |
| E775 | On the person's page the agent taps Log, an outcome, and the one button ("Save and change to Trying to reach"); the stage change fails after the log saved; the note reads like a do-not-contact request | Choosing the outcome shows the stage step in the same sheet, with the suggestion core's own table gives before the save (`activitySignal` and `suggestStageFromOutcome`, the functions the log route answers with). The one button saves the log (client `noteId`), then the follow-up, then the stage: 3 taps from the bar to a confirmed stage. If the stage change is refused after the log saved, the sheet stays open and says "Call logged (left voicemail). The stage did not change: <the server's reason>"; if the answer was lost (a network error, a 5xx) it says "We could not confirm the stage change", never "did not change"; the button stays enabled, is named for what is left ("Change to …"), and a retry makes no second note. A call with no outcome suggests nothing and the button is "Save". A note the server reads as a do-not-contact request does not change the stage by that tap: the sheet asks first, and "No" returns to the saved log with the stage still to confirm. | E776 | The stage step opens on a 390 × 664 phone | Top to bottom: what was logged, the suggested choice (its definition and why; the "does not mean" line is under "Another stage…"), "Keep <current>", "Long-term follow-up" when offered, "Another stage…", ONE line saying what the choice will do with "Keeps running: <plan>" and Stop under it, the follow-up in one row, then the one primary button pinned above the safe area and described by that line. The suggested choice, the effect line and the follow-up are inside the sheet's visible body without scrolling; when a sentence runs to three lines or more (a long plan name) the follow-up is one scroll of the sheet's body away with the button still on screen. No toast repeats the step's first line. Tests: `StageStep.test.tsx` (order, once), `j10-stage-flow.spec.ts` (`expectAtOpen`; emulated iPhone 13 on Chromium) | R-STG-3 |
